GB/TASK-020 DIED 2 SECONDS AFTER LAUNCH - TOOLING_FAILURE in scripts/dispatch.ps1, NOT a builder fault and not an ADR-002 issue. grok refused with "the argument '--single <PROMPT>' cannot be used with '[PROMPT]'" and the 566-byte log was the whole session. ROOT-CAUSED WITH MEASUREMENT, NOT INFERENCE: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (which `powershell -File` launches, NOT the pwsh 7 an interactive shell may report) does not escape embedded double quotes when building a native command line, so the target's CRT argv parser re-splits the argument at an unbalanced quote run. I probed it with an argv-printing stub on the REAL 3392-char prompt (42 double quotes): ARGC=6 unescaped versus ARGC=5 escaped - the prompt arrived as TWO argv entries, the first consumed by -p as --single and the SECOND landing as the positional [PROMPT], which is precisely what grok complained about. The split fell mid-ATLAS-excerpt. Three wrong hypotheses were killed by measurement first (a bad -p/--single signature: disproved, a short prompt through the identical shape returns OK; an array-valued \$Prompt: disproved, \$IdentityOverride is "" for GB; pwsh-7 behaviour: disproved, pwsh PRESERVES the quotes and 5.1 STRIPS them). WHY IT PASSED FOUR TIMES BEFORE: the defect is CONTENT-DEPENDENT - GB succeeded on TASK-001/004/008/010 because the ATLAS project-map section varies per task and the index was rebuilt at 27faec6 just before this wave. A latent intermittent that had been waiting, not a regression introduced by anything this session. FIX APPLIED (scripts/** is ORCH pack territory): \$PromptArg escapes embedded quotes for every CLI that takes the prompt as an ARGUMENT, applied at all three call sites including the detached launcher, which reads the prompt from a file and therefore still needed it - a file solves BOM and newlines, not argv splitting. Escaping verified CONTENT-PRESERVING, not merely count-correct: {"control_version": 1 arrives with real quotes and no literal backslash-quote over-escaping, which matters because a mangled control-block spec is exactly what produced S5's invalid devteam-control block on TASK-003. THE FIX ALSO COVERS claude/S5, which passes its prompt as a trailing positional too and carries the IDENTICAL latent bug - S5 simply has not hit an unbalanced-quote region yet, and its prompt includes the same ATLAS pack that broke grok; leaving a known landmine for a future wave when a proven fix was in hand would have been the wrong call. codex is exempt and always was: it routes the prompt through stdin, the same principle applied one layer earlier. ADR-002 section 5 permits builder permission-bypass on product paths ONLY 'where the territory firewall is active for that unit (DEVTEAM_UNIT set), WHICH MECHANICALLY CONFINES WRITES to the task's Owned_Paths'. dispatch.ps1 sets DEVTEAM_UNIT for every builder, so the letter of the parenthetical is satisfied - but for grok/codex nothing reads it, so the mechanical confinement the exception exists to secure does not exist. TASK-020 writes packages/db/** and TASK-018 writes .github/** + infra/ci/** - both product/enforcement paths under ADR-002 section 2(1) and Amendment A. So both dispatches fall OUTSIDE the carve-out. THIS IS PRE-EXISTING AND I AM THE ONE WHO KEPT MISSING IT: TASK-001 (packages/**, services/**), TASK-004 (.github + infra/ci), TASK-008 (infra/ci), TASK-010 (packages/policy - a PROTECTED path) and TASK-009 (three package.json + lockfile) were all dispatched this way and merged. Five occurrences. I reviewed every one of them and audited territory each time without noticing that the audit WAS the only control - I recorded 'territory CLEAN' five times as though it were verification of a mechanism, when it was in fact the mechanism. CX's codex invocation is the same substantive position via a different flag (-s danger-full-access), which is additionally NOT one of the three tokens CAN-03 greps for (bypassPermissions, acceptEdits, --dangerously-skip-permissions) - so the banned-mode scanner would not catch it even on an enforcement surface. That grep-coverage gap is a second finding, independent of the first. Only S5 is genuinely confined, because it runs the literal claude CLI which does load the hooks. EMPIRICAL RISK IS LOW BUT UNEARNED: all five merged builder tasks were territory-clean at review, so no out-of-territory write has actually occurred. That is builders behaving well plus ORCH review catching what it could, not a control. DECISION REQUEST FOR ALISTER (protocol section 7 - concrete options, with a recommendation, not an open question). OPTION A: amend ADR-002 section 5 to cover non-hook-capable builder CLIs, accepting the risk explicitly with post-hoc territory audit named as the compensating control - cheapest, but it legitimises the gap by writing it down rather than closing it. OPTION B: strip the bypass flags from builder invocations in dispatch.ps1 - most faithful to the ban, but headless grok/codex will hang on their first permission prompt, which ends unattended dispatch for both units. OPTION C: route all packages/** work to S5, the only confined unit - not viable, since directive section 3 bars S5 from protected paths and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. OPTION D (RECOMMENDED): add a CLI-agnostic mechanical control - a pre-commit hook installed into each builder worktree that rejects any staged path outside the claimed task's Owned_Paths, enforced by git rather than by the harness. That makes ADR-002 section 5's premise TRUE for every CLI instead of only for S5, closes the gap for grok, codex and any future unit at once, and needs no change to the ban. It is a new task on scripts/** + hooks/** (DEVDEPARTMENT infrastructure, not builder territory, so ORCH-authored) and would gate future builder dispatch. Recommend D as the durable fix with A as an interim amendment recording the accepted risk until D ships, so work is not frozen in the meantime - but that interim acceptance is Alister's call to make, not mine, which is exactly why I stopped instead of dispatching and mentioning it afterwards. GB/TASK-020 DIED 2 SECONDS AFTER LAUNCH - TOOLING_FAILURE in scripts/dispatch.ps1, NOT a builder fault and not an ADR-002 issue. grok refused with "the argument '--single <PROMPT>' cannot be used with '[PROMPT]'" and the 566-byte log was the whole session. ROOT-CAUSED WITH MEASUREMENT, NOT INFERENCE: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (which `powershell -File` launches, NOT the pwsh 7 an interactive shell may report) does not escape embedded double quotes when building a native command line, so the target's CRT argv parser re-splits the argument at an unbalanced quote run. I probed it with an argv-printing stub on the REAL 3392-char prompt (42 double quotes): ARGC=6 unescaped versus ARGC=5 escaped - the prompt arrived as TWO argv entries, the first consumed by -p as --single and the SECOND landing as the positional [PROMPT], which is precisely what grok complained about. The split fell mid-ATLAS-excerpt. Three wrong hypotheses were killed by measurement first (a bad -p/--single signature: disproved, a short prompt through the identical shape returns OK; an array-valued \$Prompt: disproved, \$IdentityOverride is "" for GB; pwsh-7 behaviour: disproved, pwsh PRESERVES the quotes and 5.1 STRIPS them). WHY IT PASSED FOUR TIMES BEFORE: the defect is CONTENT-DEPENDENT - GB succeeded on TASK-001/004/008/010 because the ATLAS project-map section varies per task and the index was rebuilt at 27faec6 just before this wave. A latent intermittent that had been waiting, not a regression introduced by anything this session. FIX APPLIED (scripts/** is ORCH pack territory): \$PromptArg escapes embedded quotes for every CLI that takes the prompt as an ARGUMENT, applied at all three call sites including the detached launcher, which reads the prompt from a file and therefore still needed it - a file solves BOM and newlines, not argv splitting. Escaping verified CONTENT-PRESERVING, not merely count-correct: {"control_version": 1 arrives with real quotes and no literal backslash-quote over-escaping, which matters because a mangled control-block spec is exactly what produced S5's invalid devteam-control block on TASK-003. THE FIX ALSO COVERS claude/S5, which passes its prompt as a trailing positional too and carries the IDENTICAL latent bug - S5 simply has not hit an unbalanced-quote region yet, and its prompt includes the same ATLAS pack that broke grok; leaving a known landmine for a future wave when a proven fix was in hand would have been the wrong call. codex is exempt and always was: it routes the prompt through stdin, the same principle applied one layer earlier. GB RE-DISPATCHED and confirmed alive (launched clean, no error where it previously died in 2s, still running). This is the FIFTH dispatch.ps1 defect found on this project and should be synced upstream to the DEVDEPARTMENT pack with the other four. REASON: scripts/dispatch.ps1 line 87 launches grok with --permission-mode bypassPermissions, and line 427's own comment states that neither grok nor codex reads .claude/settings.json, so hooks/territory-firewall.js NEVER FIRES for them. ADR-002 section 5 permits builder permission-bypass on product paths ONLY 'where the territory firewall is active for that unit (DEVTEAM_UNIT set), WHICH MECHANICALLY CONFINES WRITES to the task's Owned_Paths'. dispatch.ps1 sets DEVTEAM_UNIT for every builder, so the letter of the parenthetical is satisfied - but for grok/codex nothing reads it, so the mechanical confinement the exception exists to secure does not exist. TASK-020 writes packages/db/** and TASK-018 writes .github/** + infra/ci/** - both product/enforcement paths under ADR-002 section 2(1) and Amendment A. So both dispatches fall OUTSIDE the carve-out. THIS IS PRE-EXISTING AND I AM THE ONE WHO KEPT MISSING IT: TASK-001 (packages/**, services/**), TASK-004 (.github + infra/ci), TASK-008 (infra/ci), TASK-010 (packages/policy - a PROTECTED path) and TASK-009 (three package.json + lockfile) were all dispatched this way and merged. Five occurrences. I reviewed every one of them and audited territory each time without noticing that the audit WAS the only control - I recorded 'territory CLEAN' five times as though it were verification of a mechanism, when it was in fact the mechanism. CX's codex invocation is the same substantive position via a different flag (-s danger-full-access), which is additionally NOT one of the three tokens CAN-03 greps for (bypassPermissions, acceptEdits, --dangerously-skip-permissions) - so the banned-mode scanner would not catch it even on an enforcement surface. That grep-coverage gap is a second finding, independent of the first. Only S5 is genuinely confined, because it runs the literal claude CLI which does load the hooks. EMPIRICAL RISK IS LOW BUT UNEARNED: all five merged builder tasks were territory-clean at review, so no out-of-territory write has actually occurred. That is builders behaving well plus ORCH review catching what it could, not a control. DECISION REQUEST FOR ALISTER (protocol section 7 - concrete options, with a recommendation, not an open question). OPTION A: amend ADR-002 section 5 to cover non-hook-capable builder CLIs, accepting the risk explicitly with post-hoc territory audit named as the compensating control - cheapest, but it legitimises the gap by writing it down rather than closing it. OPTION B: strip the bypass flags from builder invocations in dispatch.ps1 - most faithful to the ban, but headless grok/codex will hang on their first permission prompt, which ends unattended dispatch for both units. OPTION C: route all packages/** work to S5, the only confined unit - not viable, since directive section 3 bars S5 from protected paths and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. OPTION D (RECOMMENDED): add a CLI-agnostic mechanical control - a pre-commit hook installed into each builder worktree that rejects any staged path outside the claimed task's Owned_Paths, enforced by git rather than by the harness. That makes ADR-002 section 5's premise TRUE for every CLI instead of only for S5, closes the gap for grok, codex and any future unit at once, and needs no change to the ban. It is a new task on scripts/** + hooks/** (DEVDEPARTMENT infrastructure, not builder territory, so ORCH-authored) and would gate future builder dispatch. Recommend D as the durable fix with A as an interim amendment recording the accepted risk until D ships, so work is not frozen in the meantime - but that interim acceptance is Alister's call to make, not mine, which is exactly why I stopped instead of dispatching and mentioning it afterwards. ROOT-CAUSED WITH MEASUREMENT, NOT INFERENCE: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (which `powershell -File` launches, NOT the pwsh 7 an interactive shell may report) does not escape embedded double quotes when building a native command line, so the target's CRT argv parser re-splits the argument at an unbalanced quote run. I probed it with an argv-printing stub on the REAL 3392-char prompt (42 double quotes): ARGC=6 unescaped versus ARGC=5 escaped - the prompt arrived as TWO argv entries, the first consumed by -p as --single and the SECOND landing as the positional [PROMPT], which is precisely what grok complained about. The split fell mid-ATLAS-excerpt. Three wrong hypotheses were killed by measurement first (a bad -p/--single signature: disproved, a short prompt through the identical shape returns OK; an array-valued \$Prompt: disproved, \$IdentityOverride is "" for GB; pwsh-7 behaviour: disproved, pwsh PRESERVES the quotes and 5.1 STRIPS them). WHY IT PASSED FOUR TIMES BEFORE: the defect is CONTENT-DEPENDENT - GB succeeded on TASK-001/004/008/010 because the ATLAS project-map section varies per task and the index was rebuilt at 27faec6 just before this wave. A latent intermittent that had been waiting, not a regression introduced by anything this session. Escaping verified CONTENT-PRESERVING, not merely count-correct: {"control_version": 1 arrives with real quotes and no literal backslash-quote over-escaping, which matters because a mangled control-block spec is exactly what produced S5's invalid devteam-control block on TASK-003. CX's codex invocation is the same substantive position via a different flag (-s danger-full-access), which is additionally NOT one of the three tokens CAN-03 greps for (bypassPermissions, acceptEdits, --dangerously-skip-permissions) - so the banned-mode scanner would not catch it even on an enforcement surface. DECISION REQUEST FOR ALISTER (protocol section 7 - concrete options, with a recommendation, not an open question). OPTION A: amend ADR-002 section 5 to cover non-hook-capable builder CLIs, accepting the risk explicitly with post-hoc territory audit named as the compensating control - cheapest, but it legitimises the gap by writing it down rather than closing it. OPTION B: strip the bypass flags from builder invocations in dispatch.ps1 - most faithful to the ban, but headless grok/codex will hang on their first permission prompt, which ends unattended dispatch for both units. OPTION C: route all packages/** work to S5, the only confined unit - not viable, since directive section 3 bars S5 from protected paths and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. OPTION D (RECOMMENDED): add a CLI-agnostic mechanical control - a pre-commit hook installed into each builder worktree that rejects any staged path outside the claimed task's Owned_Paths, enforced by git rather than by the harness. That makes ADR-002 section 5's premise TRUE for every CLI instead of only for S5, closes the gap for grok, codex and any future unit at once, and needs no change to the ban. It is a new task on scripts/** + hooks/** (DEVDEPARTMENT infrastructure, not builder territory, so ORCH-authored) and would gate future builder dispatch. Recommend D as the durable fix with A as an interim amendment recording the accepted risk until D ships, so work is not frozen in the meantime - but that interim acceptance is Alister's call to make, not mine, which is exactly why I stopped instead of dispatching and mentioning it afterwards. THE ADR-004 QUESTION IS ANSWERED AND THE ANSWER IS THE BAD ONE: `action_render` is CALLER-SUPPLIED, not derived. issue.ts:110 does requireNonEmpty(request.actionRender) - a pass-through validated only for non-emptiness - and the tests assert the pass-through on purpose. So the digest binds payload->execution while render->payload stays unbound, which is precisely the hole ADR-004 was written to close. **IT IS NOT REWORK AND NOT GB'S FAULT.** AC1 asks only that the row CARRY the render, and carrying it is exactly what GB built; ADR-004 was authored after that session had already started and could not reach it. I put that position in writing - in the pre-review note and in ADR-004's Consequences - BEFORE seeing the implementation, specifically so that confirming the finding could not become a retroactive goalpost move, and I am holding to it. GB/TASK-020 DIED 2 SECONDS AFTER LAUNCH - TOOLING_FAILURE in scripts/dispatch.ps1, NOT a builder fault and not an ADR-002 issue. grok refused with "the argument '--single <PROMPT>' cannot be used with '[PROMPT]'" and the 566-byte log was the whole session. ROOT-CAUSED WITH MEASUREMENT, NOT INFERENCE: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (which `powershell -File` launches, NOT the pwsh 7 an interactive shell may report) does not escape embedded double quotes when building a native command line, so the target's CRT argv parser re-splits the argument at an unbalanced quote run. I probed it with an argv-printing stub on the REAL 3392-char prompt (42 double quotes): ARGC=6 unescaped versus ARGC=5 escaped - the prompt arrived as TWO argv entries, the first consumed by -p as --single and the SECOND landing as the positional [PROMPT], which is precisely what grok complained about. The split fell mid-ATLAS-excerpt. Three wrong hypotheses were killed by measurement first (a bad -p/--single signature: disproved, a short prompt through the identical shape returns OK; an array-valued \$Prompt: disproved, \$IdentityOverride is "" for GB; pwsh-7 behaviour: disproved, pwsh PRESERVES the quotes and 5.1 STRIPS them). WHY IT PASSED FOUR TIMES BEFORE: the defect is CONTENT-DEPENDENT - GB succeeded on TASK-001/004/008/010 because the ATLAS project-map section varies per task and the index was rebuilt at 27faec6 just before this wave. A latent intermittent that had been waiting, not a regression introduced by anything this session. FIX APPLIED (scripts/** is ORCH pack territory): \$PromptArg escapes embedded quotes for every CLI that takes the prompt as an ARGUMENT, applied at all three call sites including the detached launcher, which reads the prompt from a file and therefore still needed it - a file solves BOM and newlines, not argv splitting. Escaping verified CONTENT-PRESERVING, not merely count-correct: {"control_version": 1 arrives with real quotes and no literal backslash-quote over-escaping, which matters because a mangled control-block spec is exactly what produced S5's invalid devteam-control block on TASK-003. THE FIX ALSO COVERS claude/S5, which passes its prompt as a trailing positional too and carries the IDENTICAL latent bug - S5 simply has not hit an unbalanced-quote region yet, and its prompt includes the same ATLAS pack that broke grok; leaving a known landmine for a future wave when a proven fix was in hand would have been the wrong call. codex is exempt and always was: it routes the prompt through stdin, the same principle applied one layer earlier. GB RE-DISPATCHED and confirmed alive (launched clean, no error where it previously died in 2s, still running). REASON: scripts/dispatch.ps1 line 87 launches grok with --permission-mode bypassPermissions, and line 427's own comment states that neither grok nor codex reads .claude/settings.json, so hooks/territory-firewall.js NEVER FIRES for them. ADR-002 section 5 permits builder permission-bypass on product paths ONLY 'where the territory firewall is active for that unit (DEVTEAM_UNIT set), WHICH MECHANICALLY CONFINES WRITES to the task's Owned_Paths'. dispatch.ps1 sets DEVTEAM_UNIT for every builder, so the letter of the parenthetical is satisfied - but for grok/codex nothing reads it, so the mechanical confinement the exception exists to secure does not exist. TASK-020 writes packages/db/** and TASK-018 writes .github/** + infra/ci/** - both product/enforcement paths under ADR-002 section 2(1) and Amendment A. So both dispatches fall OUTSIDE the carve-out. THIS IS PRE-EXISTING AND I AM THE ONE WHO KEPT MISSING IT: TASK-001 (packages/**, services/**), TASK-004 (.github + infra/ci), TASK-008 (infra/ci), TASK-010 (packages/policy - a PROTECTED path) and TASK-009 (three package.json + lockfile) were all dispatched this way and merged. Five occurrences. I reviewed every one of them and audited territory each time without noticing that the audit WAS the only control - I recorded 'territory CLEAN' five times as though it were verification of a mechanism, when it was in fact the mechanism. CX's codex invocation is the same substantive position via a different flag (-s danger-full-access), which is additionally NOT one of the three tokens CAN-03 greps for (bypassPermissions, acceptEdits, --dangerously-skip-permissions) - so the banned-mode scanner would not catch it even on an enforcement surface. That grep-coverage gap is a second finding, independent of the first. Only S5 is genuinely confined, because it runs the literal claude CLI which does load the hooks. EMPIRICAL RISK IS LOW BUT UNEARNED: all five merged builder tasks were territory-clean at review, so no out-of-territory write has actually occurred. That is builders behaving well plus ORCH review catching what it could, not a control. DECISION REQUEST FOR ALISTER (protocol section 7 - concrete options, with a recommendation, not an open question). OPTION A: amend ADR-002 section 5 to cover non-hook-capable builder CLIs, accepting the risk explicitly with post-hoc territory audit named as the compensating control - cheapest, but it legitimises the gap by writing it down rather than closing it. OPTION B: strip the bypass flags from builder invocations in dispatch.ps1 - most faithful to the ban, but headless grok/codex will hang on their first permission prompt, which ends unattended dispatch for both units. OPTION C: route all packages/** work to S5, the only confined unit - not viable, since directive section 3 bars S5 from protected paths and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. OPTION D (RECOMMENDED): add a CLI-agnostic mechanical control - a pre-commit hook installed into each builder worktree that rejects any staged path outside the claimed task's Owned_Paths, enforced by git rather than by the harness. That makes ADR-002 section 5's premise TRUE for every CLI instead of only for S5, closes the gap for grok, codex and any future unit at once, and needs no change to the ban. It is a new task on scripts/** + hooks/** (DEVDEPARTMENT infrastructure, not builder territory, so ORCH-authored) and would gate future builder dispatch. Recommend D as the durable fix with A as an interim amendment recording the accepted risk until D ships, so work is not frozen in the meantime - but that interim acceptance is Alister's call to make, not mine, which is exactly why I stopped instead of dispatching and mentioning it afterwards. GB/TASK-020 DIED 2 SECONDS AFTER LAUNCH - TOOLING_FAILURE in scripts/dispatch.ps1, NOT a builder fault and not an ADR-002 issue. grok refused with "the argument '--single <PROMPT>' cannot be used with '[PROMPT]'" and the 566-byte log was the whole session. ROOT-CAUSED WITH MEASUREMENT, NOT INFERENCE: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (which `powershell -File` launches, NOT the pwsh 7 an interactive shell may report) does not escape embedded double quotes when building a native command line, so the target's CRT argv parser re-splits the argument at an unbalanced quote run. I probed it with an argv-printing stub on the REAL 3392-char prompt (42 double quotes): ARGC=6 unescaped versus ARGC=5 escaped - the prompt arrived as TWO argv entries, the first consumed by -p as --single and the SECOND landing as the positional [PROMPT], which is precisely what grok complained about. The split fell mid-ATLAS-excerpt. Three wrong hypotheses were killed by measurement first (a bad -p/--single signature: disproved, a short prompt through the identical shape returns OK; an array-valued \$Prompt: disproved, \$IdentityOverride is "" for GB; pwsh-7 behaviour: disproved, pwsh PRESERVES the quotes and 5.1 STRIPS them). WHY IT PASSED FOUR TIMES BEFORE: the defect is CONTENT-DEPENDENT - GB succeeded on TASK-001/004/008/010 because the ATLAS project-map section varies per task and the index was rebuilt at 27faec6 just before this wave. A latent intermittent that had been waiting, not a regression introduced by anything this session. FIX APPLIED (scripts/** is ORCH pack territory): \$PromptArg escapes embedded quotes for every CLI that takes the prompt as an ARGUMENT, applied at all three call sites including the detached launcher, which reads the prompt from a file and therefore still needed it - a file solves BOM and newlines, not argv splitting. Escaping verified CONTENT-PRESERVING, not merely count-correct: {"control_version": 1 arrives with real quotes and no literal backslash-quote over-escaping, which matters because a mangled control-block spec is exactly what produced S5's invalid devteam-control block on TASK-003. THE FIX ALSO COVERS claude/S5, which passes its prompt as a trailing positional too and carries the IDENTICAL latent bug - S5 simply has not hit an unbalanced-quote region yet, and its prompt includes the same ATLAS pack that broke grok; leaving a known landmine for a future wave when a proven fix was in hand would have been the wrong call. codex is exempt and always was: it routes the prompt through stdin, the same principle applied one layer earlier. GB RE-DISPATCHED and confirmed alive (launched clean, no error where it previously died in 2s, still running). DECISION REQUEST FOR ALISTER (protocol section 7 - concrete options, with a recommendation, not an open question). OPTION A: amend ADR-002 section 5 to cover non-hook-capable builder CLIs, accepting the risk explicitly with post-hoc territory audit named as the compensating control - cheapest, but it legitimises the gap by writing it down rather than closing it. OPTION B: strip the bypass flags from builder invocations in dispatch.ps1 - most faithful to the ban, but headless grok/codex will hang on their first permission prompt, which ends unattended dispatch for both units. OPTION C: route all packages/** work to S5, the only confined unit - not viable, since directive section 3 bars S5 from protected paths and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. OPTION D (RECOMMENDED): add a CLI-agnostic mechanical control - a pre-commit hook installed into each builder worktree that rejects any staged path outside the claimed task's Owned_Paths, enforced by git rather than by the harness. That makes ADR-002 section 5's premise TRUE for every CLI instead of only for S5, closes the gap for grok, codex and any future unit at once, and needs no change to the ban. It is a new task on scripts/** + hooks/** (DEVDEPARTMENT infrastructure, not builder territory, so ORCH-authored) and would gate future builder dispatch. Recommend D as the durable fix with A as an interim amendment recording the accepted risk until D ships, so work is not frozen in the meantime - but that interim acceptance is Alister's call to make, not mine, which is exactly why I stopped instead of dispatching and mentioning it afterwards. GB/TASK-020 DIED 2 SECONDS AFTER LAUNCH - TOOLING_FAILURE in scripts/dispatch.ps1, NOT a builder fault and not an ADR-002 issue. grok refused with "the argument '--single <PROMPT>' cannot be used with '[PROMPT]'" and the 566-byte log was the whole session. ROOT-CAUSED WITH MEASUREMENT, NOT INFERENCE: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (which `powershell -File` launches, NOT the pwsh 7 an interactive shell may report) does not escape embedded double quotes when building a native command line, so the target's CRT argv parser re-splits the argument at an unbalanced quote run. I probed it with an argv-printing stub on the REAL 3392-char prompt (42 double quotes): ARGC=6 unescaped versus ARGC=5 escaped - the prompt arrived as TWO argv entries, the first consumed by -p as --single and the SECOND landing as the positional [PROMPT], which is precisely what grok complained about. The split fell mid-ATLAS-excerpt. Three wrong hypotheses were killed by measurement first (a bad -p/--single signature: disproved, a short prompt through the identical shape returns OK; an array-valued \$Prompt: disproved, \$IdentityOverride is "" for GB; pwsh-7 behaviour: disproved, pwsh PRESERVES the quotes and 5.1 STRIPS them). WHY IT PASSED FOUR TIMES BEFORE: the defect is CONTENT-DEPENDENT - GB succeeded on TASK-001/004/008/010 because the ATLAS project-map section varies per task and the index was rebuilt at 27faec6 just before this wave. A latent intermittent that had been waiting, not a regression introduced by anything this session. FIX APPLIED (scripts/** is ORCH pack territory): \$PromptArg escapes embedded quotes for every CLI that takes the prompt as an ARGUMENT, applied at all three call sites including the detached launcher, which reads the prompt from a file and therefore still needed it - a file solves BOM and newlines, not argv splitting. Escaping verified CONTENT-PRESERVING, not merely count-correct: {"control_version": 1 arrives with real quotes and no literal backslash-quote over-escaping, which matters because a mangled control-block spec is exactly what produced S5's invalid devteam-control block on TASK-003. THE FIX ALSO COVERS claude/S5, which passes its prompt as a trailing positional too and carries the IDENTICAL latent bug - S5 simply has not hit an unbalanced-quote region yet, and its prompt includes the same ATLAS pack that broke grok; leaving a known landmine for a future wave when a proven fix was in hand would have been the wrong call. codex is exempt and always was: it routes the prompt through stdin, the same principle applied one layer earlier. DECISION REQUEST FOR ALISTER (protocol section 7 - concrete options, with a recommendation, not an open question). OPTION A: amend ADR-002 section 5 to cover non-hook-capable builder CLIs, accepting the risk explicitly with post-hoc territory audit named as the compensating control - cheapest, but it legitimises the gap by writing it down rather than closing it. OPTION B: strip the bypass flags from builder invocations in dispatch.ps1 - most faithful to the ban, but headless grok/codex will hang on their first permission prompt, which ends unattended dispatch for both units. OPTION C: route all packages/** work to S5, the only confined unit - not viable, since directive section 3 bars S5 from protected paths and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. OPTION D (RECOMMENDED): add a CLI-agnostic mechanical control - a pre-commit hook installed into each builder worktree that rejects any staged path outside the claimed task's Owned_Paths, enforced by git rather than by the harness. That makes ADR-002 section 5's premise TRUE for every CLI instead of only for S5, closes the gap for grok, codex and any future unit at once, and needs no change to the ban. It is a new task on scripts/** + hooks/** (DEVDEPARTMENT infrastructure, not builder territory, so ORCH-authored) and would gate future builder dispatch. GB/TASK-020 DIED 2 SECONDS AFTER LAUNCH - TOOLING_FAILURE in scripts/dispatch.ps1, NOT a builder fault and not an ADR-002 issue. grok refused with "the argument '--single <PROMPT>' cannot be used with '[PROMPT]'" and the 566-byte log was the whole session. ROOT-CAUSED WITH MEASUREMENT, NOT INFERENCE: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (which `powershell -File` launches, NOT the pwsh 7 an interactive shell may report) does not escape embedded double quotes when building a native command line, so the target's CRT argv parser re-splits the argument at an unbalanced quote run. I probed it with an argv-printing stub on the REAL 3392-char prompt (42 double quotes): ARGC=6 unescaped versus ARGC=5 escaped - the prompt arrived as TWO argv entries, the first consumed by -p as --single and the SECOND landing as the positional [PROMPT], which is precisely what grok complained about. The split fell mid-ATLAS-excerpt. Three wrong hypotheses were killed by measurement first (a bad -p/--single signature: disproved, a short prompt through the identical shape returns OK; an array-valued \$Prompt: disproved, \$IdentityOverride is "" for GB; pwsh-7 behaviour: disproved, pwsh PRESERVES the quotes and 5.1 STRIPS them). WHY IT PASSED FOUR TIMES BEFORE: the defect is CONTENT-DEPENDENT - GB succeeded on TASK-001/004/008/010 because the ATLAS project-map section varies per task and the index was rebuilt at 27faec6 just before this wave. A latent intermittent that had been waiting, not a regression introduced by anything this session. FIX APPLIED (scripts/** is ORCH pack territory): \$PromptArg escapes embedded quotes for every CLI that takes the prompt as an ARGUMENT, applied at all three call sites including the detached launcher, which reads the prompt from a file and therefore still needed it - a file solves BOM and newlines, not argv splitting. Escaping verified CONTENT-PRESERVING, not merely count-correct: {"control_version": 1 arrives with real quotes and no literal backslash-quote over-escaping, which matters because a mangled control-block spec is exactly what produced S5's invalid devteam-control block on TASK-003. THE FIX ALSO COVERS claude/S5, which passes its prompt as a trailing positional too and carries the IDENTICAL latent bug - S5 simply has not hit an unbalanced-quote region yet, and its prompt includes the same ATLAS pack that broke grok; leaving a known landmine for a future wave when a proven fix was in hand would have been the wrong call. codex is exempt and always was: it routes the prompt through stdin, the same principle applied one layer earlier. GB RE-DISPATCHED and confirmed alive (launched clean, no error where it previously died in 2s, still running). This is the FIFTH dispatch.ps1 defect found on this project and should be synced upstream to the DEVDEPARTMENT pack with the other four. THIS IS PRE-EXISTING AND I AM THE ONE WHO KEPT MISSING IT: TASK-001 (packages/**, services/**), TASK-004 (.github + infra/ci), TASK-008 (infra/ci), TASK-010 (packages/policy - a PROTECTED path) and TASK-009 (three package.json + lockfile) were all dispatched this way and merged. Five occurrences. I reviewed every one of them and audited territory each time without noticing that the audit WAS the only control - I recorded 'territory CLEAN' five times as though it were verification of a mechanism, when it was in fact the mechanism. CX's codex invocation is the same substantive position via a different flag (-s danger-full-access), which is additionally NOT one of the three tokens CAN-03 greps for (bypassPermissions, acceptEdits, --dangerously-skip-permissions) - so the banned-mode scanner would not catch it even on an enforcement surface. That grep-coverage gap is a second finding, independent of the first. Only S5 is genuinely confined, because it runs the literal claude CLI which does load the hooks. EMPIRICAL RISK IS LOW BUT UNEARNED: all five merged builder tasks were territory-clean at review, so no out-of-territory write has actually occurred. That is builders behaving well plus ORCH review catching what it could, not a control. DECISION REQUEST FOR ALISTER (protocol section 7 - concrete options, with a recommendation, not an open question). OPTION A: amend ADR-002 section 5 to cover non-hook-capable builder CLIs, accepting the risk explicitly with post-hoc territory audit named as the compensating control - cheapest, but it legitimises the gap by writing it down rather than closing it. OPTION B: strip the bypass flags from builder invocations in dispatch.ps1 - most faithful to the ban, but headless grok/codex will hang on their first permission prompt, which ends unattended dispatch for both units. OPTION C: route all packages/** work to S5, the only confined unit - not viable, since directive section 3 bars S5 from protected paths and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. OPTION D (RECOMMENDED): add a CLI-agnostic mechanical control - a pre-commit hook installed into each builder worktree that rejects any staged path outside the claimed task's Owned_Paths, enforced by git rather than by the harness. That makes ADR-002 section 5's premise TRUE for every CLI instead of only for S5, closes the gap for grok, codex and any future unit at once, and needs no change to the ban. It is a new task on scripts/** + hooks/** (DEVDEPARTMENT infrastructure, not builder territory, so ORCH-authored) and would gate future builder dispatch. Recommend D as the durable fix with A as an interim amendment recording the accepted risk until D ships, so work is not frozen in the meantime - but that interim acceptance is Alister's call to make, not mine, which is exactly why I stopped instead of dispatching and mentioning it afterwards. Prior note follows. Plan v2.0 - STATUS SCAN 2026-08-15T12:57Z (ORCH opus-4-8): ONE BUILDER DOWN AND RECOVERED, ONE HEALTHY. GB/TASK-020 DIED 2 SECONDS AFTER LAUNCH - TOOLING_FAILURE in scripts/dispatch.ps1, NOT a builder fault and not an ADR-002 issue. grok refused with "the argument '--single <PROMPT>' cannot be used with '[PROMPT]'" and the 566-byte log was the whole session. ROOT-CAUSED WITH MEASUREMENT, NOT INFERENCE: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (which `powershell -File` launches, NOT the pwsh 7 an interactive shell may report) does not escape embedded double quotes when building a native command line, so the target's CRT argv parser re-splits the argument at an unbalanced quote run. I probed it with an argv-printing stub on the REAL 3392-char prompt (42 double quotes): ARGC=6 unescaped versus ARGC=5 escaped - the prompt arrived as TWO argv entries, the first consumed by -p as --single and the SECOND landing as the positional [PROMPT], which is precisely what grok complained about. The split fell mid-ATLAS-excerpt. Three wrong hypotheses were killed by measurement first (a bad -p/--single signature: disproved, a short prompt through the identical shape returns OK; an array-valued \$Prompt: disproved, \$IdentityOverride is "" for GB; pwsh-7 behaviour: disproved, pwsh PRESERVES the quotes and 5.1 STRIPS them). WHY IT PASSED FOUR TIMES BEFORE: the defect is CONTENT-DEPENDENT - GB succeeded on TASK-001/004/008/010 because the ATLAS project-map section varies per task and the index was rebuilt at 27faec6 just before this wave. A latent intermittent that had been waiting, not a regression introduced by anything this session. FIX APPLIED (scripts/** is ORCH pack territory): \$PromptArg escapes embedded quotes for every CLI that takes the prompt as an ARGUMENT, applied at all three call sites including the detached launcher, which reads the prompt from a file and therefore still needed it - a file solves BOM and newlines, not argv splitting. Escaping verified CONTENT-PRESERVING, not merely count-correct: {"control_version": 1 arrives with real quotes and no literal backslash-quote over-escaping, which matters because a mangled control-block spec is exactly what produced S5's invalid devteam-control block on TASK-003. THE FIX ALSO COVERS claude/S5, which passes its prompt as a trailing positional too and carries the IDENTICAL latent bug - S5 simply has not hit an unbalanced-quote region yet, and its prompt includes the same ATLAS pack that broke grok; leaving a known landmine for a future wave when a proven fix was in hand would have been the wrong call. codex is exempt and always was: it routes the prompt through stdin, the same principle applied one layer earlier. GB RE-DISPATCHED and confirmed alive (launched clean, no error where it previously died in 2s, still running). This is the FIFTH dispatch.ps1 defect found on this project and should be synced upstream to the DEVDEPARTMENT pack with the other four. VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED: hooks/test-territory-precommit.js drives REAL git commits in a throwaway repo with registered worktrees - 10/10, asserting the catch direction (out-of-territory, PLAN.md under strict, another task's dossier, unit/worktree mismatch) and the PASS direction separately (in-territory file, own dossier, ORCH in main checkout), because a hook that rejected everything would satisfy the catch half alone; plus three fail-closed states (no active task, unregistered worktree, unreadable PLAN.md). TASK-020's artifacts confirmed present on master. THREE TASKS ARE ACTIONABLE AND ALL THREE BUILDERS ARE IDLE: TASK-011 (S5) and TASK-013 (CX) became eligible the moment TASK-020 merged - both Depends_On are done and both retain their branches for section 10a resume - and TASK-018 (CX) is in rework. That is the same idle-capacity finding as the 21:20Z and 08:00Z scans and it is a dispatch away from resolved. ORCH DECISION TAKEN THIS SCAN, the item I left open at review: `.claude/**` coverage. I widened CLAUDE.md's protected-paths list from the vague 'all settings and subagent configs' to name `hooks/**`, `.claude/**` and `.codex/**` explicitly. Reason: both are control-plane surface where an edit silently weakens an enforcement mechanism instead of breaking a test. `hooks/` now holds territory-precommit.js, the mechanical territory control Amendment B installed, and `.claude/commands/**` defines the review procedure itself - so under the old wording the review standard could have been edited without review, and the control I just built to close one gap sat outside the list that protects controls. I fixed the SOURCE list first rather than just the gate, because TASK-018's AC1 is defined as 'every protected path listed in CLAUDE.md'; changing the gate alone would have left the authoritative list and the enforcement disagreeing. Two acceptance criteria were consequently ADDED to TASK-018 and both are labelled in the task as scope additions with rationale, not as reinterpretations of what CX delivered - CX's narrower reading was CORRECT for the text as it stood, and the text was the defect. BACKLOG ASSIGNED per protocol section 8 from REVIEW.md evidence, so neither is unclaimable when its dependency lands: TASK-014 (OIK-022 atomic consume, N8, protected) -> GB, on two grounds - GB is 6 reviews / 6 first-pass / 0 rework against CX's 10 / 5 / 3 and this is the most security-critical function in the platform so far, and CX authors TASK-013 in the same territory so a different unit on the consume statement puts fresh eyes on the invariant rather than the same reading twice. TASK-012 (audit redaction, not protected) -> S5, the opposite call for the opposite reason: it sits directly on top of the TASK-011 writer it must not bypass, so continuity of context beats fresh eyes there. Both are sequenced behind their predecessors by Depends_On, so neither shared territory is ever concurrent. Still TBD: TASK-015/016/017 (all gated) and TASK-019 (OIK-042 OpenSandbox, still HELD pending Alister confirming the Addendum B reconciliation - unchanged, and it blocks nothing because no E5 work is in flight). NEXT: dispatch - S5 to TASK-011 and CX to TASK-018. Deliberately NOT dispatching CX to TASK-013 in the same wave: CX owes the TASK-018 fix, which is short and still fresh in its context, and giving one unit two active tasks would breach the one-claimed-task discipline the whole isolation model rests on. TASK-013 goes to CX on the wave after, or to GB if CX is still occupied when TASK-014 is not yet eligible. Prior note follows. Plan v2.0 - REVIEW 2026-08-15T13:20Z (ORCH opus-4-8): TASK-020 APPROVED first-pass and MERGED --no-ff 8216637 (branch deleted, wt-grok returned to master); TASK-018 REWORK, not merged, branch task/TASK-018-cx retained. **TASK-020 MERGE UNBLOCKS THE CRITICAL PATH**: TASK-011 (S5, audit writer) and TASK-013 (CX, approvals issue/bind) now have every Depends_On satisfied (TASK-009 done + TASK-020 done) and both keep their retained branches for section 10a resume. TASK-013 then unlocks TASK-014 (OIK-022 atomic consume, N8), which is the run to OIK-039 CAN-01..08 and the G-GOV gate. TASK-020 verification was independent at every step and matched the builder exactly: fresh isolated pg16 on port 55433 with its own volume, migration under ON_ERROR_STOP=1, 8/8 with DATABASE_URL and 2-passed/6-skipped without, lint+typecheck+build all 0. Two claims got specific scrutiny rather than a green tick - AC1's RETURNING is proven by re-SELECTing through an INDEPENDENT pool and asserting server-generated event_id/at (values echo cannot produce), and AC2's induced write failure is a genuine server-side 22P02, which I confirmed by reading auditEvents.ts to rule out a client-side guard (runId is bound straight to $2 with no local validation). AC5's no-consumeApproval guarantee - the reason this task existed - holds by independent grep AND structurally, since persistence-surface.test.ts asserts the EXACT sorted export list, so a future status-mutating helper breaks the test instead of slipping past negative property checks; the N8 statement remains TASK-014's to author beside its own concurrency proof. Four NON-BLOCKING findings recorded because 011/013/016 build on this surface: per-call Pool create/destroy sits on the broker hot path (ADR-001 L1 audits every decision) and TASK-016 must not ship on it; withPool/requireNonEmpty duplicated verbatim across both modules; `at` is caller-settable on an append-only audit table, so TASK-011 should not surface it; and tenant_id's 'basileia' default is hardcoded in SQL duplicating the schema DEFAULT. TASK-018 REWORK IS ONE BLOCKING FINDING AND IT IS THE TASK-007 DEFECT AGAIN: the protected-path gate is DORMANT. run-local.mjs:56 invokes only test-protected-path-review.mjs (the self-test); the real gate protected-path-review.mjs runs ONLY at ci.yml:81 under `if: github.event_name == pull_request`, and this repo HAS NO REMOTE - so nothing in any automated path ever evaluates a protected diff, and a protected-path change can be committed and merged today with no gate firing. That is the precise outcome WBS OIK-003 exists to prevent, and it makes AC4's own claim that the local script is 'the operative control' untrue when a human must remember to invoke it. TASK-007 was reworked for exactly this shape - a threshold configured, correct, fail-closed when invoked, and invoked by nothing - so approving here would have retired that precedent three tasks after I set it. The rest of TASK-018 is good and says so in the findings: territory clean, CODEOWNERS covers all six protected paths with none omitted plus self-protects .github/CODEOWNERS and workflows, and I proved the gate fires in BOTH directions in a throwaway repo its harness had never seen, including a wrong-marker case that shows exact-string matching rather than truthiness. Fix is small and in-territory. Two further items recorded without demanding rework: the approval marker is SELF-ASSERTED (CI greps the PR body for a comment the author can paste - no reviewer-identity binding, so directive section 3's different-model requirement is honor-system at this layer; documented honestly in the script header, and CODEOWNERS + branch protection is the real fix once a remote exists), and an ORCH DECISION ITEM that `.claude/**` is only partly covered - settings*.json, agents/** and .codex/** yes, but commands/**, hooks/** and skills/** no. AC1 is strictly MET because CLAUDE.md's list says 'all settings and subagent configs', but slash commands and hooks are executable control-plane surface and the DEVDEPARTMENT firewall names `.claude/**` wholesale, so I must either widen both lists or write down why the narrower carve-out is deliberate. TALLIES: GB now 6 reviews / 6 first-pass / 0 rework; CX 10 / 5 / 3. NEXT: re-dispatch S5 on TASK-011 and CX on TASK-013 (both now eligible, both resume on retained branches), and CX also owes the TASK-018 fix - sequence those two so CX is not asked to hold two active tasks at once. Prior note follows. Plan v2.0 - STATUS SCAN 2026-08-15T12:57Z (ORCH opus-4-8): ONE BUILDER DOWN AND RECOVERED, ONE HEALTHY. GB/TASK-020 DIED 2 SECONDS AFTER LAUNCH - TOOLING_FAILURE in scripts/dispatch.ps1, NOT a builder fault and not an ADR-002 issue. grok refused with "the argument '--single <PROMPT>' cannot be used with '[PROMPT]'" and the 566-byte log was the whole session. ROOT-CAUSED WITH MEASUREMENT, NOT INFERENCE: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (which `powershell -File` launches, NOT the pwsh 7 an interactive shell may report) does not escape embedded double quotes when building a native command line, so the target's CRT argv parser re-splits the argument at an unbalanced quote run. I probed it with an argv-printing stub on the REAL 3392-char prompt (42 double quotes): ARGC=6 unescaped versus ARGC=5 escaped - the prompt arrived as TWO argv entries, the first consumed by -p as --single and the SECOND landing as the positional [PROMPT], which is precisely what grok complained about. The split fell mid-ATLAS-excerpt. Three wrong hypotheses were killed by measurement first (a bad -p/--single signature: disproved, a short prompt through the identical shape returns OK; an array-valued \$Prompt: disproved, \$IdentityOverride is "" for GB; pwsh-7 behaviour: disproved, pwsh PRESERVES the quotes and 5.1 STRIPS them). WHY IT PASSED FOUR TIMES BEFORE: the defect is CONTENT-DEPENDENT - GB succeeded on TASK-001/004/008/010 because the ATLAS project-map section varies per task and the index was rebuilt at 27faec6 just before this wave. A latent intermittent that had been waiting, not a regression introduced by anything this session. FIX APPLIED (scripts/** is ORCH pack territory): \$PromptArg escapes embedded quotes for every CLI that takes the prompt as an ARGUMENT, applied at all three call sites including the detached launcher, which reads the prompt from a file and therefore still needed it - a file solves BOM and newlines, not argv splitting. Escaping verified CONTENT-PRESERVING, not merely count-correct: {"control_version": 1 arrives with real quotes and no literal backslash-quote over-escaping, which matters because a mangled control-block spec is exactly what produced S5's invalid devteam-control block on TASK-003. THE FIX ALSO COVERS claude/S5, which passes its prompt as a trailing positional too and carries the IDENTICAL latent bug - S5 simply has not hit an unbalanced-quote region yet, and its prompt includes the same ATLAS pack that broke grok; leaving a known landmine for a future wave when a proven fix was in hand would have been the wrong call. codex is exempt and always was: it routes the prompt through stdin, the same principle applied one layer earlier. GB RE-DISPATCHED and confirmed alive (launched clean, no error where it previously died in 2s, still running). This is the FIFTH dispatch.ps1 defect found on this project and should be synced upstream to the DEVDEPARTMENT pack with the other four. CX/TASK-018 HEALTHY: single commit a739c74 [TASK-018], territory CLEAN - .github/CODEOWNERS, .github/workflows/ci.yml, infra/ci/{README.md,protected-path-review.mjs,run-local.mjs,test-protected-path-review.mjs} plus its own dossier, all inside Owned_Paths; zero PLAN.md commits on the branch. PLAN.md appears in git diff master..HEAD only as BASE DRIFT (master advanced past the branch point), not as an edit - the distinction matters and a diff-only reading would misreport it as a violation. THE NEW TERRITORY HOOK IS CONFIRMED WORKING IN PRODUCTION, not just in its own test harness: a739c74 was committed after hooks/territory-precommit.js went live, so git necessarily ran it, and it correctly PERMITTED an in-territory commit. The pass direction now has real-world evidence alongside the 10/10 fixture suite. Plan v2.0 - DISPATCH WAVE 3 LIVE 2026-08-15T15:10Z, GATE CLEARED FIRST: GB->TASK-020 (packages/db persistence surface for audit_events + approvals) and CX->TASK-018 (OIK-003 CODEOWNERS + protected-path CI enforcement). Both claimed by the dispatcher (control.mode=strict), both worktrees refreshed to master tip by the dispatcher itself this time (adcc07d->682328d and ->8a90042), territories disjoint (packages/db/** vs .github/** + infra/ci/**). THE HALT IS RESOLVED, NOT DEFERRED: Alister chose Option D, so hooks/territory-precommit.js now enforces Owned_Paths at the GIT layer, where every CLI is equal - a commit staging anything outside the committing unit's active territory is rejected. ADR-002 section 5's exception finally has the mechanism it always claimed. Three properties make it a control and not a restatement: it FAILS CLOSED (territory-firewall.js fails open by design because review backstops it; this hook is the only mechanical control for grok/codex, so an unresolvable state rejects), the unit is derived FROM THE WORKTREE not from DEVTEAM_UNIT (git hooks inherit ambient env, and lib.unit() maps unset to unrestricted ORCH - trusting it would let the control disable itself in exactly the case it exists to catch; the env var is now only a cross-check and disagreement is a rejection), and PLAN.md is read FROM THE MAIN CHECKOUT (a worktree copy could otherwise widen the Owned_Paths that authorise the very commit being checked). Installed once at .git/hooks/pre-commit via scripts/install_git_hooks.ps1 - git resolves hooks from the common dir, so one file covers the main checkout and every present and future worktree, and it COMPOSES with TASK-004's OIK-007 secret-scan hook rather than clobbering it. VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED: hooks/test-territory-precommit.js drives REAL git commits in a throwaway repo with registered worktrees - 10/10, asserting the catch direction (out-of-territory, PLAN.md under strict, another task's dossier, unit/worktree mismatch) and the PASS direction separately (in-territory file, own dossier, ORCH in main checkout), because a hook that rejected everything would satisfy the catch half alone; plus three fail-closed states (no active task, unregistered worktree, unreadable PLAN.md). Real scanners still green: banned-modes 8/8 + exit 0, secret-scan 11/11 + exit 0. ADR-002 AMENDMENT B records all of it, including the five prior merged tasks that ran under the gap (TASK-001/004/008/009/010 - all territory-clean at review, so no write actually escaped, but review was the ONLY control and I approved every one of them without noticing that). FOUR RESIDUAL RISKS STATED IN THE ADR RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER: (a) --no-verify bypasses the hook - this defends against drift and accident, not a hostile builder, so git log -p at review stays mandatory; (b) the hook governs COMMITS not writes - out-of-territory files can exist unstaged in a worktree, harmless to the integration branch but visible at review; (c) scripts/install_git_hooks.sh IS NOT WRITTEN, so Linux/clawsrv install is manual until it is - tracked, not done, and I am not claiming parity I did not build; (d) the CAN-03 grep still does not match codex's -s danger-full-access, a SECOND finding independent of the first and NOT closed by this work - the token list tracks Claude Code vocabulary and does not generalise across CLIs; a future change should generalise it or record why it need not. S5 remains idle by design: TASK-011 is gated behind TASK-020, and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. NEXT: /devteam-status in 30-45 min; on TASK-020 merge, re-dispatch S5 on TASK-011 and CX on TASK-013 (both branches RETAINED for section 10a resume, both will now be enforced by the new hook). Prior note follows. Plan v2.0 - DISPATCH HALTED 2026-08-15T14:30Z (ORCH opus-4-8), NOT A FAILED DISPATCH - A GOVERNANCE STOP. TASK-020 (GB) and TASK-018 (CX) were validated, territory-checked disjoint, worktrees refreshed and both dry-runs resolved correctly. I did not launch them. REASON: scripts/dispatch.ps1 line 87 launches grok with --permission-mode bypassPermissions, and line 427's own comment states that neither grok nor codex reads .claude/settings.json, so hooks/territory-firewall.js NEVER FIRES for them. ADR-002 section 5 permits builder permission-bypass on product paths ONLY 'where the territory firewall is active for that unit (DEVTEAM_UNIT set), WHICH MECHANICALLY CONFINES WRITES to the task's Owned_Paths'. dispatch.ps1 sets DEVTEAM_UNIT for every builder, so the letter of the parenthetical is satisfied - but for grok/codex nothing reads it, so the mechanical confinement the exception exists to secure does not exist. TASK-020 writes packages/db/** and TASK-018 writes .github/** + infra/ci/** - both product/enforcement paths under ADR-002 section 2(1) and Amendment A. So both dispatches fall OUTSIDE the carve-out. THIS IS PRE-EXISTING AND I AM THE ONE WHO KEPT MISSING IT: TASK-001 (packages/**, services/**), TASK-004 (.github + infra/ci), TASK-008 (infra/ci), TASK-010 (packages/policy - a PROTECTED path) and TASK-009 (three package.json + lockfile) were all dispatched this way and merged. Five occurrences. I reviewed every one of them and audited territory each time without noticing that the audit WAS the only control - I recorded 'territory CLEAN' five times as though it were verification of a mechanism, when it was in fact the mechanism. CX's codex invocation is the same substantive position via a different flag (-s danger-full-access), which is additionally NOT one of the three tokens CAN-03 greps for (bypassPermissions, acceptEdits, --dangerously-skip-permissions) - so the banned-mode scanner would not catch it even on an enforcement surface. That grep-coverage gap is a second finding, independent of the first. Only S5 is genuinely confined, because it runs the literal claude CLI which does load the hooks. EMPIRICAL RISK IS LOW BUT UNEARNED: all five merged builder tasks were territory-clean at review, so no out-of-territory write has actually occurred. That is builders behaving well plus ORCH review catching what it could, not a control. DECISION REQUEST FOR ALISTER (protocol section 7 - concrete options, with a recommendation, not an open question). OPTION A: amend ADR-002 section 5 to cover non-hook-capable builder CLIs, accepting the risk explicitly with post-hoc territory audit named as the compensating control - cheapest, but it legitimises the gap by writing it down rather than closing it. OPTION B: strip the bypass flags from builder invocations in dispatch.ps1 - most faithful to the ban, but headless grok/codex will hang on their first permission prompt, which ends unattended dispatch for both units. OPTION C: route all packages/** work to S5, the only confined unit - not viable, since directive section 3 bars S5 from protected paths and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. OPTION D (RECOMMENDED): add a CLI-agnostic mechanical control - a pre-commit hook installed into each builder worktree that rejects any staged path outside the claimed task's Owned_Paths, enforced by git rather than by the harness. That makes ADR-002 section 5's premise TRUE for every CLI instead of only for S5, closes the gap for grok, codex and any future unit at once, and needs no change to the ban. It is a new task on scripts/** + hooks/** (DEVDEPARTMENT infrastructure, not builder territory, so ORCH-authored) and would gate future builder dispatch. Recommend D as the durable fix with A as an interim amendment recording the accepted risk until D ships, so work is not frozen in the meantime - but that interim acceptance is Alister's call to make, not mine, which is exactly why I stopped instead of dispatching and mentioning it afterwards. STATE LEFT CLEAN AND READY: PLAN.md legal (validator 0 warnings); TASK-018 assigned to CX this session (a planning decision that stands regardless of the halt - it was TBD and dependency-eligible while CX sat idle, the same idle-capacity finding as the 21:20Z and 08:00Z scans); worktrees refreshed to master@adcc07d (wt-grok was stranded on the merged task/TASK-010-gb, wt-codex on the stale task/TASK-009-cx base); merged branches task/TASK-010-gb and task/TASK-009-cx deleted; task/TASK-011-s5 and task/TASK-013-cx RETAINED for section 10a resume. Nothing was claimed, so no task is left in a claimed-and-silent state. On Alister's decision, dispatch is one command: GB -> TASK-020, CX -> TASK-018, both dry-run-verified this session. Plan v2.0 - DISPATCH WAVE 3 LIVE 2026-08-15T15:10Z, GATE CLEARED FIRST: GB->TASK-020 (packages/db persistence surface for audit_events + approvals) and CX->TASK-018 (OIK-003 CODEOWNERS + protected-path CI enforcement). Both claimed by the dispatcher (control.mode=strict), both worktrees refreshed to master tip by the dispatcher itself this time (adcc07d->682328d and ->8a90042), territories disjoint (packages/db/** vs .github/** + infra/ci/**). THE HALT IS RESOLVED, NOT DEFERRED: Alister chose Option D, so hooks/territory-precommit.js now enforces Owned_Paths at the GIT layer, where every CLI is equal - a commit staging anything outside the committing unit's active territory is rejected. ADR-002 section 5's exception finally has the mechanism it always claimed. Three properties make it a control and not a restatement: it FAILS CLOSED (territory-firewall.js fails open by design because review backstops it; this hook is the only mechanical control for grok/codex, so an unresolvable state rejects), the unit is derived FROM THE WORKTREE not from DEVTEAM_UNIT (git hooks inherit ambient env, and lib.unit() maps unset to unrestricted ORCH - trusting it would let the control disable itself in exactly the case it exists to catch; the env var is now only a cross-check and disagreement is a rejection), and PLAN.md is read FROM THE MAIN CHECKOUT (a worktree copy could otherwise widen the Owned_Paths that authorise the very commit being checked). Installed once at .git/hooks/pre-commit via scripts/install_git_hooks.ps1 - git resolves hooks from the common dir, so one file covers the main checkout and every present and future worktree, and it COMPOSES with TASK-004's OIK-007 secret-scan hook rather than clobbering it. VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED: hooks/test-territory-precommit.js drives REAL git commits in a throwaway repo with registered worktrees - 10/10, asserting the catch direction (out-of-territory, PLAN.md under strict, another task's dossier, unit/worktree mismatch) and the PASS direction separately (in-territory file, own dossier, ORCH in main checkout), because a hook that rejected everything would satisfy the catch half alone; plus three fail-closed states (no active task, unregistered worktree, unreadable PLAN.md). Real scanners still green: banned-modes 8/8 + exit 0, secret-scan 11/11 + exit 0. ADR-002 AMENDMENT B records all of it, including the five prior merged tasks that ran under the gap (TASK-001/004/008/009/010 - all territory-clean at review, so no write actually escaped, but review was the ONLY control and I approved every one of them without noticing that). FOUR RESIDUAL RISKS STATED IN THE ADR RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER: (a) --no-verify bypasses the hook - this defends against drift and accident, not a hostile builder, so git log -p at review stays mandatory; (b) the hook governs COMMITS not writes - out-of-territory files can exist unstaged in a worktree, harmless to the integration branch but visible at review; (c) scripts/install_git_hooks.sh IS NOT WRITTEN, so Linux/clawsrv install is manual until it is - tracked, not done, and I am not claiming parity I did not build; (d) the CAN-03 grep still does not match codex's -s danger-full-access, a SECOND finding independent of the first and NOT closed by this work - the token list tracks Claude Code vocabulary and does not generalise across CLIs; a future change should generalise it or record why it need not. S5 remains idle by design: TASK-011 is gated behind TASK-020, and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. NEXT: /devteam-status in 30-45 min; on TASK-020 merge, re-dispatch S5 on TASK-011 and CX on TASK-013 (both branches RETAINED for section 10a resume, both will now be enforced by the new hook). Prior note follows. Plan v2.0 - DISPATCH HALTED 2026-08-15T14:30Z (ORCH opus-4-8), NOT A FAILED DISPATCH - A GOVERNANCE STOP. TASK-020 (GB) and TASK-018 (CX) were validated, territory-checked disjoint, worktrees refreshed and both dry-runs resolved correctly. I did not launch them. REASON: scripts/dispatch.ps1 line 87 launches grok with --permission-mode bypassPermissions, and line 427's own comment states that neither grok nor codex reads .claude/settings.json, so hooks/territory-firewall.js NEVER FIRES for them. ADR-002 section 5 permits builder permission-bypass on product paths ONLY 'where the territory firewall is active for that unit (DEVTEAM_UNIT set), WHICH MECHANICALLY CONFINES WRITES to the task's Owned_Paths'. dispatch.ps1 sets DEVTEAM_UNIT for every builder, so the letter of the parenthetical is satisfied - but for grok/codex nothing reads it, so the mechanical confinement the exception exists to secure does not exist. TASK-020 writes packages/db/** and TASK-018 writes .github/** + infra/ci/** - both product/enforcement paths under ADR-002 section 2(1) and Amendment A. So both dispatches fall OUTSIDE the carve-out. THIS IS PRE-EXISTING AND I AM THE ONE WHO KEPT MISSING IT: TASK-001 (packages/**, services/**), TASK-004 (.github + infra/ci), TASK-008 (infra/ci), TASK-010 (packages/policy - a PROTECTED path) and TASK-009 (three package.json + lockfile) were all dispatched this way and merged. Five occurrences. I reviewed every one of them and audited territory each time without noticing that the audit WAS the only control - I recorded 'territory CLEAN' five times as though it were verification of a mechanism, when it was in fact the mechanism. CX's codex invocation is the same substantive position via a different flag (-s danger-full-access), which is additionally NOT one of the three tokens CAN-03 greps for (bypassPermissions, acceptEdits, --dangerously-skip-permissions) - so the banned-mode scanner would not catch it even on an enforcement surface. That grep-coverage gap is a second finding, independent of the first. Only S5 is genuinely confined, because it runs the literal claude CLI which does load the hooks. EMPIRICAL RISK IS LOW BUT UNEARNED: all five merged builder tasks were territory-clean at review, so no out-of-territory write has actually occurred. That is builders behaving well plus ORCH review catching what it could, not a control. DECISION REQUEST FOR ALISTER (protocol section 7 - concrete options, with a recommendation, not an open question). OPTION A: amend ADR-002 section 5 to cover non-hook-capable builder CLIs, accepting the risk explicitly with post-hoc territory audit named as the compensating control - cheapest, but it legitimises the gap by writing it down rather than closing it. OPTION B: strip the bypass flags from builder invocations in dispatch.ps1 - most faithful to the ban, but headless grok/codex will hang on their first permission prompt, which ends unattended dispatch for both units. OPTION C: route all packages/** work to S5, the only confined unit - not viable, since directive section 3 bars S5 from protected paths and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. OPTION D (RECOMMENDED): add a CLI-agnostic mechanical control - a pre-commit hook installed into each builder worktree that rejects any staged path outside the claimed task's Owned_Paths, enforced by git rather than by the harness. That makes ADR-002 section 5's premise TRUE for every CLI instead of only for S5, closes the gap for grok, codex and any future unit at once, and needs no change to the ban. It is a new task on scripts/** + hooks/** (DEVDEPARTMENT infrastructure, not builder territory, so ORCH-authored) and would gate future builder dispatch. Recommend D as the durable fix with A as an interim amendment recording the accepted risk until D ships, so work is not frozen in the meantime - but that interim acceptance is Alister's call to make, not mine, which is exactly why I stopped instead of dispatching and mentioning it afterwards. STATE LEFT CLEAN AND READY: PLAN.md legal (validator 0 warnings); TASK-018 assigned to CX this session (a planning decision that stands regardless of the halt - it was TBD and dependency-eligible while CX sat idle, the same idle-capacity finding as the 21:20Z and 08:00Z scans); worktrees refreshed to master@adcc07d (wt-grok was stranded on the merged task/TASK-010-gb, wt-codex on the stale task/TASK-009-cx base); merged branches task/TASK-010-gb and task/TASK-009-cx deleted; task/TASK-011-s5 and task/TASK-013-cx RETAINED for section 10a resume. Nothing was claimed, so no task is left in a claimed-and-silent state. On Alister's decision, dispatch is one command: GB -> TASK-020, CX -> TASK-018, both dry-run-verified this session. Prior note follows. Plan v2.0 - STATUS SCAN 2026-08-15T12:57Z (ORCH opus-4-8): ONE BUILDER DOWN AND RECOVERED, ONE HEALTHY. GB/TASK-020 DIED 2 SECONDS AFTER LAUNCH - TOOLING_FAILURE in scripts/dispatch.ps1, NOT a builder fault and not an ADR-002 issue. grok refused with "the argument '--single <PROMPT>' cannot be used with '[PROMPT]'" and the 566-byte log was the whole session. ROOT-CAUSED WITH MEASUREMENT, NOT INFERENCE: Windows PowerShell 5.1 (which `powershell -File` launches, NOT the pwsh 7 an interactive shell may report) does not escape embedded double quotes when building a native command line, so the target's CRT argv parser re-splits the argument at an unbalanced quote run. I probed it with an argv-printing stub on the REAL 3392-char prompt (42 double quotes): ARGC=6 unescaped versus ARGC=5 escaped - the prompt arrived as TWO argv entries, the first consumed by -p as --single and the SECOND landing as the positional [PROMPT], which is precisely what grok complained about. The split fell mid-ATLAS-excerpt. Three wrong hypotheses were killed by measurement first (a bad -p/--single signature: disproved, a short prompt through the identical shape returns OK; an array-valued \$Prompt: disproved, \$IdentityOverride is "" for GB; pwsh-7 behaviour: disproved, pwsh PRESERVES the quotes and 5.1 STRIPS them). WHY IT PASSED FOUR TIMES BEFORE: the defect is CONTENT-DEPENDENT - GB succeeded on TASK-001/004/008/010 because the ATLAS project-map section varies per task and the index was rebuilt at 27faec6 just before this wave. A latent intermittent that had been waiting, not a regression introduced by anything this session. FIX APPLIED (scripts/** is ORCH pack territory): \$PromptArg escapes embedded quotes for every CLI that takes the prompt as an ARGUMENT, applied at all three call sites including the detached launcher, which reads the prompt from a file and therefore still needed it - a file solves BOM and newlines, not argv splitting. Escaping verified CONTENT-PRESERVING, not merely count-correct: {"control_version": 1 arrives with real quotes and no literal backslash-quote over-escaping, which matters because a mangled control-block spec is exactly what produced S5's invalid devteam-control block on TASK-003. THE FIX ALSO COVERS claude/S5, which passes its prompt as a trailing positional too and carries the IDENTICAL latent bug - S5 simply has not hit an unbalanced-quote region yet, and its prompt includes the same ATLAS pack that broke grok; leaving a known landmine for a future wave when a proven fix was in hand would have been the wrong call. codex is exempt and always was: it routes the prompt through stdin, the same principle applied one layer earlier. GB RE-DISPATCHED and confirmed alive (launched clean, no error where it previously died in 2s, still running). This is the FIFTH dispatch.ps1 defect found on this project and should be synced upstream to the DEVDEPARTMENT pack with the other four. CX/TASK-018 HEALTHY: single commit a739c74 [TASK-018], territory CLEAN - .github/CODEOWNERS, .github/workflows/ci.yml, infra/ci/{README.md,protected-path-review.mjs,run-local.mjs,test-protected-path-review.mjs} plus its own dossier, all inside Owned_Paths; zero PLAN.md commits on the branch. PLAN.md appears in git diff master..HEAD only as BASE DRIFT (master advanced past the branch point), not as an edit - the distinction matters and a diff-only reading would misreport it as a violation. THE NEW TERRITORY HOOK IS CONFIRMED WORKING IN PRODUCTION, not just in its own test harness: a739c74 was committed after hooks/territory-precommit.js went live, so git necessarily ran it, and it correctly PERMITTED an in-territory commit. The pass direction now has real-world evidence alongside the 10/10 fixture suite. CLOCK HYGIENE - MY ERROR, SECOND OCCURRENCE ON THIS PROJECT: the wave-3 dispatch note stamped 15:10Z was local SAST (+02:00) mislabeled as Z; true UTC at dispatch was 12:45Z and is 12:57Z as this note is written. The 06:24Z review note flagged this exact mistake once already and I repeated it. Timestamps in THIS note are true UTC. S5/TASK-011 and CX/TASK-013 unchanged - both pending, both gated behind TASK-020, both branches (task/TASK-011-s5 @ d86c4ba, task/TASK-013-cx @ 65c0c24) RETAINED for section 10a resume. No blocked tasks awaiting triage; no stale heartbeats; validator clean throughout. Prior note follows. Plan v2.0 - DISPATCH HALTED 2026-08-15T14:30Z (ORCH opus-4-8), NOT A FAILED DISPATCH - A GOVERNANCE STOP. TASK-020 (GB) and TASK-018 (CX) were validated, territory-checked disjoint, worktrees refreshed and both dry-runs resolved correctly. I did not launch them. REASON: scripts/dispatch.ps1 line 87 launches grok with --permission-mode bypassPermissions, and line 427's own comment states that neither grok nor codex reads .claude/settings.json, so hooks/territory-firewall.js NEVER FIRES for them. ADR-002 section 5 permits builder permission-bypass on product paths ONLY 'where the territory firewall is active for that unit (DEVTEAM_UNIT set), WHICH MECHANICALLY CONFINES WRITES to the task's Owned_Paths'. dispatch.ps1 sets DEVTEAM_UNIT for every builder, so the letter of the parenthetical is satisfied - but for grok/codex nothing reads it, so the mechanical confinement the exception exists to secure does not exist. TASK-020 writes packages/db/** and TASK-018 writes .github/** + infra/ci/** - both product/enforcement paths under ADR-002 section 2(1) and Amendment A. So both dispatches fall OUTSIDE the carve-out. THIS IS PRE-EXISTING AND I AM THE ONE WHO KEPT MISSING IT: TASK-001 (packages/**, services/**), TASK-004 (.github + infra/ci), TASK-008 (infra/ci), TASK-010 (packages/policy - a PROTECTED path) and TASK-009 (three package.json + lockfile) were all dispatched this way and merged. Five occurrences. I reviewed every one of them and audited territory each time without noticing that the audit WAS the only control - I recorded 'territory CLEAN' five times as though it were verification of a mechanism, when it was in fact the mechanism. CX's codex invocation is the same substantive position via a different flag (-s danger-full-access), which is additionally NOT one of the three tokens CAN-03 greps for (bypassPermissions, acceptEdits, --dangerously-skip-permissions) - so the banned-mode scanner would not catch it even on an enforcement surface. That grep-coverage gap is a second finding, independent of the first. Only S5 is genuinely confined, because it runs the literal claude CLI which does load the hooks. EMPIRICAL RISK IS LOW BUT UNEARNED: all five merged builder tasks were territory-clean at review, so no out-of-territory write has actually occurred. That is builders behaving well plus ORCH review catching what it could, not a control. DECISION REQUEST FOR ALISTER (protocol section 7 - concrete options, with a recommendation, not an open question). OPTION A: amend ADR-002 section 5 to cover non-hook-capable builder CLIs, accepting the risk explicitly with post-hoc territory audit named as the compensating control - cheapest, but it legitimises the gap by writing it down rather than closing it. OPTION B: strip the bypass flags from builder invocations in dispatch.ps1 - most faithful to the ban, but headless grok/codex will hang on their first permission prompt, which ends unattended dispatch for both units. OPTION C: route all packages/** work to S5, the only confined unit - not viable, since directive section 3 bars S5 from protected paths and S5 draws on the human's own Anthropic window. OPTION D (RECOMMENDED): add a CLI-agnostic mechanical control - a pre-commit hook installed into each builder worktree that rejects any staged path outside the claimed task's Owned_Paths, enforced by git rather than by the harness. That makes ADR-002 section 5's premise TRUE for every CLI instead of only for S5, closes the gap for grok, codex and any future unit at once, and needs no change to the ban. It is a new task on scripts/** + hooks/** (DEVDEPARTMENT infrastructure, not builder territory, so ORCH-authored) and would gate future builder dispatch. Recommend D as the durable fix with A as an interim amendment recording the accepted risk until D ships, so work is not frozen in the meantime - but that interim acceptance is Alister's call to make, not mine, which is exactly why I stopped instead of dispatching and mentioning it afterwards. THE FINDING, VERIFIED BY ORCH NOT ACCEPTED ON CLAIM: packages/db/src/database.ts exports a Database class with exactly seven methods (close, getCapability, listCapabilities, upsertCapability, getRoleGrant, listRoleGrants, upsertRoleGrant) - all scoped to capabilities/role_grants. There is no audit_events write path, no approvals write path, and no generic query()/pool escape hatch. TASK-006 AC1 ('no raw SQL outside packages/db', WBS OIK-014) forbids the obvious workaround, and TASK-009 deliberately declined to give packages/audit its own pg dependency ('Add NO external runtime dependency'). So TASK-011 and TASK-013 are INOPERABLE as scoped: their Owned_Paths cover only their own src/test, and the persistence method they must call does not exist anywhere they are allowed to write. WHY TASK-009 DID NOT PREVENT THIS: TASK-009 solved MANIFEST contention (who may edit pnpm-lock.yaml and the three package.json files) and solved it correctly - the wiring it produced is sound and stays merged. It did not solve API-SURFACE readiness, which is a different axis: declaring @oikonomos/db as a dependency does not make it export the function the consumer needs. The generalised lesson, now stronger than the TASK-005/006/007 version: a task is only dispatchable when BOTH its manifest grants AND the API surface it must call already exist; if the consumer's data-layer method is missing, that method needs its own single-owner task sequenced ahead via Depends_On, exactly as protocol section 4 prescribes for shared files. # Project Plan

Coordination blackboard for ORCH (Claude Code), GB (Grok Build), CX (Codex AI), S5 (Sonnet 5 headless).
Rules: `AGENTS.md` (summary) and `docs/COORDINATION_PROTOCOL.md` (authoritative).
Status lifecycle: `pending → claimed → in_progress → needs_review → done`, `blocked` from claimed/in_progress. Builders never set `done`.
control.mode is **strict**: builders never edit PLAN.md — the dispatcher claims, the supervisor applies state from your fenced `devteam-control` block (docs/CONTROL.md).
