### TASK-003
**Title:** OIK-017/018 — packages/shared: canonical JSON + sha256 action digest
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** S5
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §1, §4; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E3 OIK-017, OIK-018; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Build_Handover_Package_v1.0.md §4.3
**Owned_Paths:** packages/shared/src/**, packages/shared/test/**, packages/shared/README.md
**Depends_On:** TASK-001
**Description:** The single canonical-JSON + digest implementation for the whole platform (N10 — no reimplementation will ever be permitted elsewhere, so precision here is everything; this is why the task goes to the careful-reading builder). Implement per Handover §4.3: canonical form = sorted keys, no whitespace, UTF-8, numbers in shortest round-trip form; `action_digest = sha256(canonicalJson({toolName, input, destination}))`. Document the byte-level spec in packages/shared/README.md (WBS OIK-018 "documented byte-level spec") — edge cases explicitly: unicode normalization stance, -0 vs 0, exponent form, null vs absent keys, nested arrays/objects, non-finite numbers (reject). Node crypto only — zero runtime dependencies. packages/shared's package.json/tsconfig belong to TASK-001; if a manifest change is essential, block with OWNERSHIP_CONFLICT rather than editing it.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] Property tests prove stability under key order, unicode content, number forms, and nesting — same value object always yields byte-identical canonical JSON and digest (WBS OIK-017 acceptance)
- [ ] Digest reproducible across processes: test spawns a child process and compares digests (WBS OIK-018 "reproducible across processes")
- [ ] No collisions across the fixture set; fixtures contain no credential-like values (WBS OIK-017; N4)
- [ ] Non-finite numbers and undefined reject with a typed error, not silent coercion (fail-closed posture, directive §4)
- [ ] README byte-level spec covers every edge case above (WBS OIK-018)
- [ ] Zero runtime dependencies; strict types, no `any` (CLAUDE.md)
**Branch:** task/TASK-003-s5
**Started_At:** 2026-08-14T15:08:45Z

**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** GB
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §4, §6; docs/decisions/ADR-002-permission-bypass-ban-scope.md Amendment A; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E1 OIK-004, OIK-007
**Owned_Paths:** infra/ci/lib/**, infra/ci/test-banned-modes.mjs, infra/ci/test-secret-scan.mjs
**Depends_On:** TASK-004
**Description:** Backlog (TBD — **GB or CX only, never S5**: protected path infra/ci, different-model review rule per directive §3). Raised by the opus-4-8 reviewer as a non-blocking fast-follow on TASK-004 and confirmed live by ORCH at 21:10Z: `infra/ci/lib/walk.mjs` walks the whole tree without honoring .gitignore, so a local full-tree run of `banned-modes.mjs` and `secret-scan.mjs` both exit 1 on `.devteam/runs/*.log` — machine-local builder transcripts that legitimately contain bypass tokens and API-key-shaped fixtures. CI on a clean checkout and the pre-commit staged path are both unaffected, which is why this is not a TASK-004 rework, but a scanner that cries wolf locally is a scanner developers learn to ignore — and that is how a real finding gets missed. ORCH has separately added `.devteam/` to .gitignore (the untracked-but-not-ignored state was a genuine N4 exposure: one `git add .` would have committed credential-shaped content); this task makes the scanners agree with git about what is part of the repo. Implement gitignore-awareness in walk.mjs (`git check-ignore --stdin` when inside a work tree, with a documented fallback skip-list when git is absent so CI containers without git still behave). Do NOT weaken Amendment A's enforcement surfaces: an ignored path is skipped because it is not part of the repo, never because it is carved out.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] `node infra/ci/banned-modes.mjs` and `node infra/ci/secret-scan.mjs` both exit 0 on the real working tree with .devteam/ present (the live failure this task exists to fix)
- [ ] A planted violation in a TRACKED enforcement path is still caught — proven by test, so the fix cannot have been achieved by over-skipping (WBS OIK-004/007)
- [ ] A planted violation in an IGNORED path is skipped, and the test asserts the reason is gitignore-membership, not an allowlist carve-out (ADR-002 Amendment A)
- [ ] Documented fallback when git is unavailable; behavior identical on a clean checkout with no .devteam/
- [ ] Existing self-tests (6/6 banned-modes, 9/9 secret-scan) stay green

### TASK-011
**Title:** OIK-025 — packages/audit: append-only writer
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** S5
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §4, §5; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E3 OIK-025; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Platform_Synthesis_Spec_v0.1.md §5.1 (audit_events); docs/decisions/ADR-001-broker-enforcement-point.md R3
**Owned_Paths:** packages/audit/src/**, packages/audit/test/**, packages/audit/vitest.config.ts
**Depends_On:** TASK-009, TASK-020
**Description:** The append-only audit writer over the `audit_events` table TASK-002 created. Not a protected path, which is why it is assignable to S5. Two properties carry the weight: **every decision is written, including denials** — a denial that is not audited is indistinguishable from an action that never happened — and **a write failure fails the action closed** (ADR-001 R3), so the caller must be able to tell "audited" from "not audited" and refuse to proceed on the latter. Never swallow a write error. TASK-002 already enforces append-only in the database via `DO INSTEAD NOTHING` rules; your job is the writer side, and your tests should prove the property holds end-to-end from this package rather than re-testing the SQL in isolation. Integration tests run against the local compose Postgres (`infra/compose/docker-compose.local.yml`, apply `infra/postgres/migrations/001_schema_v1.up.sql`); follow TASK-006's pattern of skipping cleanly when `DATABASE_URL` is unset so the suite stays green on a machine with no database. **N4 is absolute here:** no credentials in fixtures, and audit payloads are exactly the place a careless test fixture leaks one.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] Every decision is written including denials — test asserts a denial produces a row, not just an allow (WBS OIK-025 "Every decision written incl. denials")
- [ ] A write failure fails the action closed: the writer surfaces the failure to its caller rather than returning success or swallowing it (WBS OIK-025 "write failure fails the action closed"; ADR-001 R3)
- [ ] UPDATE and DELETE against a written event are provable no-ops when exercised through this package (WBS OIK-013 property, verified from the writer side)
- [ ] Integration tests pass against the compose Postgres and skip cleanly with no `DATABASE_URL` (TASK-006 precedent)
- [ ] No credential-like values in any fixture; `node infra/ci/secret-scan.mjs` exits 0 (N4)

- [ ] Nonces are unguessable (CSPRNG, not Math.random or a counter) and unique under concurrent issuance
- [ ] Integration tests pass against the compose Postgres; skip cleanly without `DATABASE_URL`

- ORCH PRE-REVIEW NOTE 2026-08-15T14:35Z (raised mid-flight from external evidence; NOT a rework instruction, and NOT reachable by GB this session — this is a CHECK TO PERFORM AT REVIEW): **`action_render` provenance is unspecified across the entire spec set.** A grep of docs/architecture, docs/decisions and specs finds exactly ONE mention — a SQL comment at Synthesis Spec line 214, `action_render text NOT NULL, -- human-readable: destination + content/param diff`. Handover §4.3, the GOVERNING document under CLAUDE.md precedence, says nothing about the render at all, and this task's AC1 requires only that the row "carries" it. THE EXPOSURE: `action_digest` binds the PAYLOAD (toolName, input, destination); it does NOT bind the RENDER. A payload mutated after grant is caught by digest mismatch (OIK-023/TASK-015). But a render that is MISLEADING AT ISSUANCE produces a perfectly valid digest and a human who approves something other than what executes — the yes rests on the render, not the hash, so an unfaithful render defeats the approval gate without ever tripping it. Not hypothetical: Grok Bot self-reports this exact shape, its approval render being model-authored prose that "can summarize, omit, or be precise", quality being "the model's judgment that turn". If OIKONOMOS accepts a caller-supplied free-text render it inherits the identical weakness at the identical point. AT REVIEW establish which GB built: (a) render DERIVED deterministically from the payload — the Synthesis comment's "destination + content/param diff" points this way — in which case OIKONOMOS is genuinely ahead and an ADR merely ratifies it; or (b) render accepted as caller-supplied text, in which case the gate is only as honest as its caller and that must close before TASK-016 wires the broker. An ADR is owed either way (docs/decisions/** protected, ORCH-authored, same shape as ADR-003) BEFORE TASK-015 and TASK-016, the consumers. If (b), the fix probably belongs with TASK-015's invalidation work rather than as rework here — GB built to the AC as written, so the AC is the defect, not the builder.
- ORCH PRE-REVIEW DETERMINATION 2026-08-15T14:00Z — the ADR-004 question above is ANSWERED, and the answer is (b): **`action_render` is CALLER-SUPPLIED, not derived.** Evidence: `packages/approvals/src/issue.ts:20` and `:36` declare `readonly actionRender: string` on the request and response types; `:110` does `requireNonEmpty(request.actionRender, "actionRender")` — a pass-through validated only for non-emptiness, never computed from the payload; and the tests assert the pass-through deliberately (`expect(row!.actionRender).toBe(request.actionRender)` in both issue.test.ts and issue.integration.test.ts). So the digest binds payload→execution while render→payload stays unbound, which is exactly the exposure ADR-004 exists to close. **THIS IS NOT REWORK AND NOT A FAULT OF GB.** AC1 requires only that the row *carries* digest, render, destination, nonce and expiry, and carrying it is precisely what was built; ADR-004 was authored after this session had already started and could not reach it. The acceptance criterion is the defect, not the builder — I recorded that position in the pre-review note above and in ADR-004's Consequences section BEFORE seeing the implementation, and I am holding to it now that the finding is confirmed. Remediation is already sequenced onto TASK-015, which carries an explicit ADR-004 acceptance criterion, and TASK-016 carries a matching one forbidding the broker from composing, accepting or passing a render. Ordering works out: 013 -> 014 -> 015 -> 016, so the signature changes in 015 BEFORE the broker wires it in 016. The reviewer should therefore NOT hold this task on the render; the remaining review question is whether AC1-AC5 are met as written. Expect churn in TASK-014's test helpers when 015 changes the issue signature — that is anticipated, not a regression.
- APPROVED first-pass (ORCH opus-4-8, 2026-08-15T14:30Z). All five ACs hold under independent verification. AC2 persist-before-wait is GENUINELY PROVEN rather than asserted after the fact: issue.test.ts wraps the store so insert writes the row and then BLOCKS on a manually-released gate, spins until the row is visible, and at that instant asserts the issue promise has NOT settled - reversing the order in issue.ts fails it two different ways. AC3 N10 holds behaviourally and not merely by regex: bind.ts delegates to @oikonomos/shared actionDigest, grep finds zero createHash / Math.random / JSON.stringify in src, and a test recomputes the expected digest by calling shared directly and asserts byte-equality plus key-order independence, so any divergent local implementation fails. AC4 nonce is node:crypto randomUUID (CSPRNG - no Math.random, counter or timestamp) with 16 genuinely concurrent issuances against live Postgres yielding 16 distinct nonces, backed by a uuid NOT NULL UNIQUE column so a collision would surface as a constraint violation. AC5: 16/16 under an isolated pg16, 13 passed / 3 skipped without DATABASE_URL. Quality: ZERO catch blocks in src or test (nothing swallowed), all SQL parameterised, validation before any persist, toWaitSignal throws on a surprising DB status (fail-closed), no dead code, no credential-like fixtures (RFC 6761 .test addresses only). Territory clean (11 files plus own dossier), c8b9872 verbatim, zero PLAN.md commits. Merged --no-ff 6d5e3db. (2) nonce.test.ts's 200-way uniqueness check is Promise.all(Promise.resolve(gen())), a sequential loop in a concurrency costume - harmless, because AC4's genuine proof is the 16-way Postgres test, but it should not be mistaken for a concurrency test. ON action_render: confirmed caller-supplied exactly as recorded in the pre-review determination above; NOT held against this task, with remediation sequenced onto TASK-015 per ADR-004.

### TASK-014
**Title:** OIK-022 — packages/approvals: verify + atomic consume (N8) ⚑ protected
**Status:** done
**Assigned_To:** GB
**Priority:** critical
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §3, §4, §6; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E3 OIK-022; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Build_Handover_Package_v1.0.md §4.3; docs/decisions/ADR-001-broker-enforcement-point.md CAN-06
**Owned_Paths:** packages/approvals/src/**, packages/approvals/test/**, packages/approvals/vitest.config.ts, packages/db/src/approvals.ts
**Depends_On:** TASK-013
**Description:** **ORCH assignment 2026-08-15T13:40Z (protocol §8, from REVIEW.md evidence): GB.** Protected path, so GB or CX only, NEVER S5. Two reasons for GB over CX: on the evidence GB is 6 reviews / 6 first-pass approvals / 0 rework while CX is 10 / 5 / 3, and this is the single most security-critical function in the platform so far — that record is the one to spend on it. Second, CX authors TASK-013 in this same territory, and having a different unit write the consume statement puts fresh eyes on the invariant rather than the same reading twice. Sequenced behind TASK-013 by Depends_On, so the shared territory is never concurrent. **ORCH territory grant 2026-08-15T14:05Z:** `packages/db/src/approvals.ts` is in your Owned_Paths. TASK-020 creates that file with `insertApproval` + `getApprovalByNonce` and deliberately does NOT write the consume statement — you author `consumeApproval` there yourself. The reason is specific: the atomic-consume invariant and the concurrency test that proves it must be authored together, because a consume statement written by one task and tested by another is exactly the seam a fail-open defect survives (the same failure shape ADR-003 records for the max_tier ceiling/floor trap). You may modify the two functions TASK-020 delivered only if your tests show them wrong — say so explicitly in Progress_Notes if you do. Territorially safe: the audit chain (TASK-011/012) owns `packages/db/src/auditEvents.ts`, never this file, and the approvals chain TASK-013→014→015 is serialised by Depends_On so it is never concurrent with itself. The single most security-critical function in the platform so far. Consumption is **one atomic SQL statement** — the exact form is pinned in Handover §4.3 (`UPDATE approvals SET status='consumed', consumed_at=now() WHERE nonce=$1 AND status='granted' AND expires_at>now() AND consumed_at IS NULL`) — and the action runs **only if the row count is 1**. Any read-then-write shape, any transaction that checks and then updates, any application-level lock is a defect regardless of whether the test suite catches it: the invariant is that two concurrent consumers of the same nonce produce exactly one success at the *database* level. The concurrency test must be genuinely concurrent (parallel connections racing the same nonce), not sequential calls that merely look like a race. Sequenced behind TASK-013 in the same territory.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] Concurrent consume attempts on one nonce yield exactly one success — proven by a DB-level test with genuinely parallel connections (WBS OIK-022 "Concurrent consume attempts ⇒ exactly one success (DB-level test)")
- [ ] Consumption is one atomic statement whose row count gates the action; row count 1 or the action does not run (N8, Handover §4.3)
- [ ] Expiry is honoured — an expired but still `granted` approval cannot be consumed (WBS OIK-022 "expiry honoured")
- [ ] Replay of a consumed nonce is denied and leaves `status='consumed'` unchanged (ADR-001 CAN-06)
- [ ] Integration tests pass against the compose Postgres; skip cleanly without `DATABASE_URL`
**Branch:** task/TASK-014-gb
**Started_At:** 2026-08-15T14:31:37Z
**Progress_Notes:**
- [2026-08-15T14:55:27Z] [SV:GB] consumeApproval is the pinned Handover §4.3 UPDATE; verifyAndConsume allows the action only on rowCount 1. 16-way parallel consume and 16 pre-connected clients racing one nonce each yield exactly one success; expiry and CAN-06 replay deny. Barrel index.ts not touched (out of territory); persistence-surface reservation test now fails as expected.
**Artifacts:** packages/db/src/approvals.ts, packages/approvals/src/consume.ts, packages/approvals/src/store.ts, packages/approvals/src/index.ts, packages/approvals/test/consume.test.ts, packages/approvals/test/consume.integration.test.ts, packages/approvals/test/consume-sql.test.ts, packages/approvals/test/helpers.ts, packages/approvals/test/issue.test.ts, dossiers/TASK-014.md

THE PINNED SQL IS VERBATIM - I compared it clause by clause MYSELF against Handover 4.3 before delegating, and CONSUME_APPROVAL_SQL (packages/db/src/approvals.ts:212) is character-identical apart from the trailing semicolon: SET status='consumed', consumed_at=now(), WHERE nonce=$1 AND status='granted' AND expires_at>now() AND consumed_at IS NULL. No missing predicate, no extra predicate, no altered column or literal. Single pool.query, no BEGIN/COMMIT, no SELECT, no FOR UPDATE, no advisory lock; RETURNING is appended but changes neither which rows match nor the single-statement property. TWO MUTATION PROOFS, which is what separates this from a suite that merely passes: (1) dropping `status='granted' AND expires_at>now()` from the statement made three integration tests fail, so every predicate is LOAD-BEARING rather than decorative; (2) replacing the implementation with a check-then-update (SELECT, 20ms delay, unconditional UPDATE) made the parallel test fail Expected 1 / Received 9 - the concurrency test genuinely DETECTS the read-then-write shape the spec forbids. AC1 the race is real: 16 promises are constructed inside Array.from and left in flight before any await (not TASK-013's Promise.resolve costume), each consumeApproval opens its OWN Pool so these are 16 separate physical connections, and a second tighter test pre-establishes the connections to remove setup jitter; both assert EXACTLY ONE success and RACERS-1 zeros. AC2 row count gates the action - rowCount 1 is the only path carrying an approval (discriminated union, so a caller cannot reach the row on denial), rowCount ?? 0 defaults to deny on pg's nullable field, and rowCount > 1 THROWS rather than proceeding. AC3 expiry honoured, asserted with state (status still 'granted', consumedAt still null). AC4 replay denied AND UNCHANGED - the test asserts consumedAt timestamp EQUALITY, which catches a replay that silently re-stamps the row rather than merely checking status. AC5 37/37 under isolated pg16, 25 passed / 12 skipped without DATABASE_URL, clean skips. Quality: zero catch blocks (try/finally for cleanup only), every statement parameterised, nonce UUID-validated before the pool opens with a proven consumeCalls===0 on rejection, no credentials. TASK-013 REGRESSION CHECK CLEAN: issue.test.ts and helpers.ts are ADDITIVE ONLY - two lines adding a consume member to inline stubs so they satisfy the widened interface, zero deletions, zero assertion changes, all 9 pre-existing tests still pass. Territory clean, c8b9872 preflight in the committed dossier, zero PLAN.md commits. Merged --no-ff 2e9cb67. **NON-BLOCKING FINDING THAT IS MY SCOPING CONSEQUENCE, NOT GB'S DEFECT - and the THIRD time this session a territory boundary forced a workaround:** packages/approvals/src/store.ts reaches the db implementation by FILESYSTEM PATH rather than the package barrel - `new URL("../../db/dist/approvals.js", import.meta.url)` followed by a dynamic import. GB documented it as a territory workaround and it fails closed with a clear error when packages/db is unbuilt, which is the right handling of a constraint it could not remove: I granted packages/db/src/approvals.ts but NOT packages/db/src/index.ts, so consumeApproval could not be exported through the barrel. It is nonetheless production code depending on a sibling package's BUILD OUTPUT by relative path - it will break under bundling or any packaging that does not preserve that layout, and it silently depends on build ordering. RE-SCOPED to TASK-015 with the barrel grant needed to fix it properly. Second non-blocking: withPool opens and tears down a Pool per consumeApproval call, i.e. a fresh TCP connect and auth on the hottest security path per invocation - same shape as the TASK-020 finding, and TASK-016 must not ship the broker on it. Third, minor: the `rowCount === 1 && rows[0] !== undefined` guard falls through to a plain denial if RETURNING ever yielded no row, converting an invariant violation into an ordinary deny instead of throwing like its rowCount > 1 sibling - fail-closed and practically unreachable given UNIQUE(nonce), but inconsistent with the sibling branch.

**Title:** OIK-023 + OIK-024 — approvals: invalidation on payload mutation, expiry sweeper ⚑ protected
**Status:** needs_review
**Assigned_To:** GB
**Priority:** high

**Owned_Paths:** packages/approvals/src/**, packages/approvals/test/**, packages/approvals/vitest.config.ts, packages/db/src/index.ts
**Depends_On:** TASK-014
**Description:** Backlog (**GB or CX only, never S5**). Two closely-related finishers, kept in one task because they share the same territory and neither is a full session alone. (1) OIK-023: if the action payload changes after approval was granted, the recomputed digest no longer matches, the approval transitions to `invalidated`, and a new approval is required (ADR-001 CAN-07 — this is the defence against "approve a harmless action, then swap the payload"). (2) OIK-024: an idempotent sweeper moving expired `pending` approvals to `expired`. Idempotent means running it twice changes nothing the second time, and running it concurrently with itself does not double-transition.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] **ADDED 2026-08-15T15:10Z (ORCH), re-scoped from TASK-014 - my scoping consequence, not GB's defect.** `packages/approvals/src/store.ts` currently reaches the db implementation by FILESYSTEM PATH - `new URL("../../db/dist/approvals.js", import.meta.url)` plus a dynamic import - because I granted TASK-014 `packages/db/src/approvals.ts` but not the barrel, so `consumeApproval` could not be exported properly. GB handled the constraint correctly (documented, fails closed when packages/db is unbuilt) but it is production code depending on a sibling package's BUILD OUTPUT by relative path: it breaks under bundling or any packaging that does not preserve that layout, and it silently depends on build ordering. **NARROW GRANT:** `packages/db/src/index.ts` is added to your Owned_Paths for the sole purpose of exporting `consumeApproval` (and its result type) from the barrel. Then switch `store.ts` - and the integration tests that import `../../db/src/approvals.js` across the package boundary - to import from `@oikonomos/db`. Add nothing else to the barrel.
- [ ] **ADDED 2026-08-15T14:50Z (ORCH), per ADR-004:** `action_render` is DERIVED from the same canonical payload the digest covers and is never caller-supplied. If TASK-013 shipped a render parameter, remove it here and compute the render in `packages/shared` beside `actionDigest` from the identical canonical object (N10 — one canonicalization, not two). Carry the discriminating test ADR-004 §5 requires: a change to ANY digest-covered field must change the render, which fails for any implementation where the render is decorative, constant, or caller-controlled. Rationale: the digest binds payload→execution but NOT render→payload, so a render that is misleading at issuance yields a valid digest, a clean consume and a complete audit trail of an action the human was never shown. This sits with you rather than as TASK-013 rework because TASK-013's AC was written without the constraint — the criterion was the defect, not the builder.
- [ ] Digest mismatch after grant ⇒ status becomes `invalidated` and a new approval is required (WBS OIK-023; ADR-001 CAN-07)
- [ ] An invalidated approval cannot subsequently be consumed, even with a valid nonce
- [ ] Expired `pending` approvals transition to `expired`; the sweeper is idempotent across repeated and concurrent runs (WBS OIK-024)
- [ ] TASK-013 and TASK-014 tests still pass unchanged

- REWORK (ORCH opus-4-8, 2026-08-15T16:20Z; protected path, author GB / reviewer ORCH-opus, different-model rule satisfied). TWO NARROW FINDINGS, BOTH IN-TERRITORY. Read the next paragraph first, because it is the larger part of this review: THE SUBSTANTIVE WORK IS EXCELLENT AND SURVIVED TARGETED MUTATION ON ALL THREE REQUIREMENTS. (a) ADR-004 derived render is correct and hard-tested - the issuance API no longer accepts a render (the input-type field and its requireNonEmpty validation are both gone), issue.ts builds ONE action object and feeds it to both bindActionDigest and actionRender, render.ts canonicalises via the same @oikonomos/shared canonicalJson over the identical {toolName, input, destination} key set the digest hashes, and render.test.ts varies ALL THREE digest-covered fields rather than one, proves key-order insensitivity, and even proves a caller-sneaked actionRender property is ignored. Mutation: making actionRender() return a constant failed 2 tests. (b) Invalidation is a single atomic statement pinned the way consume is, transitions to 'invalidated', and an invalidated approval cannot be consumed even with the original matching payload; two mutations (removing the invalidate call; relaxing consume's status guard to <>'consumed') each failed the right tests. (c) Sweeper idempotence is genuinely proven - 8 promises constructed before any await, each opening its OWN Pool, total-across-runners asserted rather than per-runner, second sequential run yields zero, and dropping the status='pending' guard failed sharply. TASK-014's atomic consume is BYTE-IDENTICAL still (git diff of packages/db/src/approvals.ts is empty) - no drift. TASK-013/014 tests show zero relaxations; the one deleted assertion is the one that CANNOT survive (it asserted rejection of an empty actionRender parameter that no longer exists), and issue.test.ts's expectation was STRENGTHENED from toBe(request.actionRender) to toBe(expectedRender(request)), i.e. it now asserts derivation. Territory clean, dossier committed with preflight, zero PLAN.md commits.
- [BLOCKING 1 - FLAKY TEST, will intermittently red the merged suite] test/sweep.integration.test.ts failed 1 of 4 full-suite runs with `AssertionError: expected 13 to be 12`. Cause: EXPIRE_PENDING_SQL is unscoped (no run or tenant filter), vitest runs test FILES in parallel, and consume.integration.test.ts inserts a pending row with expiresAt in the past - so the sweep occasionally collects a foreign fixture row and the total-swept assertion overshoots. This is a test-isolation defect, NOT an idempotence failure: the production behaviour is correct and the assertion itself is sharp (the mutation proof above depends on it). It is blocking because a suite that fails a quarter of the time trains everyone to re-run rather than investigate, which is the same reasoning that made TASK-008's crying-wolf scanner worth fixing. FIX, in-territory and small: scope the assertion (or the sweep call) to the fixture's own runId, or make this file sequential. While you are there, `expect(raced.every((r) => r.expired >= 0)).toBe(true)` is vacuous - a count cannot be negative - so it asserts nothing.
- [BLOCKING 2 - ACCEPTANCE CRITERION NOT MET, and it is the one that hides drift] The added AC said, verbatim: "Then switch `store.ts` - AND THE INTEGRATION TESTS that import `../../db/src/approvals.js` across the package boundary - to import from `@oikonomos/db`." store.ts was switched correctly and completely (the dist-path dynamic import and loadConsumeApproval are gone, and consume-sql.test.ts even pins their absence). The tests were not: consume-sql.test.ts:7, consume-sql.test.ts:13 and consume.integration.test.ts:16 still import `../../db/src/approvals.js`. WHY THIS MATTERS BEYOND TIDINESS: @oikonomos/db's exports map resolves to dist/, so the code under test EXECUTES dist/ while the SQL pin test READS src/. Reviewer demonstrated the consequence - a mutation to packages/db/src/approvals.ts went completely UNDETECTED until a rebuild. The pin test therefore appears to guard the atomic consume statement while guarding a file that is not the one running. That is the exact failure class ADR-005 was written for this afternoon, sitting inside the test that exists to protect N8. Switch the three imports to @oikonomos/db so the pin reads what executes.
- [NON-BLOCKING, fix while you are in there] Dead code: `ConsumeApprovalFn` (store.ts:17-20, re-exported from index.ts) has zero consumers in src/ - it is the injection seam left over from the dynamic-import design the barrel fix replaced. Delete it.
- [NON-BLOCKING - NOT YOUR DEFECT, recorded so it is not lost] store.ts:86-91 still resolves the pg driver through `createRequire(fileURLToPath(new URL("../../db/package.json", import.meta.url)))` because pg is a dependency of @oikonomos/db and not of packages/approvals. This is materially LESS fragile than the old dist path (it anchors on a committed file present from both src and dist) and it was not in your Owned_Paths to fix properly, but it is the same class: a hardcoded relative path across a package boundary. The clean fix is either adding pg to packages/approvals or re-exporting a pool helper from @oikonomos/db, and that is a decomposition decision, not yours.
- [ORCH-OWNED, NOT REWORK - the deeper issue behind BLOCKING 2] @oikonomos/db's exports map points at ./dist/index.js and dist/ is gitignored, so moving packages/db/dist aside makes 10 of 13 approvals test files fail to load outright, and every cross-package test in this repo may be executing stale build output whenever a rebuild is skipped. That predates this task and is not GB's to fix. It is a textbook ADR-005 instance - a suite that looks like it guards src while running dist - so I am adding it as a sixth liveness assertion to TASK-021 rather than a rework item here.

**Description:** Backlog (**GB or CX only, never S5**). **ORCH assignment + RE-SEQUENCE 2026-08-15T15:10Z: CX, and now gated behind TASK-015 as well.** The dependency is not bureaucratic - I created a genuine contradiction and this resolves it. The added acceptance criterion below says the broker must not compose, accept or pass an approval render, but the CURRENT issueApproval REQUIRES actionRender (packages/approvals/src/issue.ts:20, non-optional, validated by requireNonEmpty at :110), so the broker cannot call issuance at all without passing one. TASK-016 could not satisfy its own AC until TASK-015 lands the ADR-004 remediation that removes the parameter. Building it concurrently would have produced a broker written against a signature about to change. Assigned to CX rather than GB deliberately: GB authored TASK-013, TASK-014 and now TASK-015, i.e. the whole approvals package, and the broker is the integration point where a FRESH reading of the approvals contract is worth most - a second author re-deriving how issue/consume/invalidate compose is a real check that ORCH review alone does not provide. CX is protected-path eligible (directive section 3 bars only S5) and idle. The decision handler behind `POST /v1/broker/pretooluse`, implemented as a library function here — the HTTP surface is OIK-084 in E9, so do not add a web framework. The request and the three response shapes are pinned verbatim in Handover §4.1 and the contract must match exactly, field for field. Composition: resolve the tier via `@oikonomos/policy`, verify/consume via `@oikonomos/approvals`, write the decision via `@oikonomos/audit` — every request audited, **allow or deny**. **ADR-003 is mandatory reading and is the known trap:** `role_grants.max_tier` is a ceiling, the resolver's override parameter is a floor, and wiring the column straight through fails OPEN — a role capped at T1 against a T3 capability would resolve to T3. Convert explicitly at the boundary and carry a negative test where the ceiling sits *below* the capability default. An unregistered `toolName` denies and audits as `capability.unregistered` (Handover §4.2).

When it issues an approval it supplies only the payload; the render is derived downstream. ADR-004 is mandatory reading alongside ADR-003 — this is the second trap on this path where a value that looks like a harmless parameter silently defeats a control (ADR-003: a ceiling passed as a floor fails OPEN; ADR-004: a caller-supplied render fails the human).
- [ ] All three response shapes implemented exactly per Handover §4.1 (allow; deny; deny with `reason: "approval_pending"` + approvalId), field names and types matching the contract verbatim
- [ ] Every request produces an audit event, denials included (ADR-001 L1)
- [ ] Unregistered `toolName` ⇒ deny, audited as `capability.unregistered` (Handover §4.2, fail closed)
- [ ] ADR-003 negative test present: a role grant whose ceiling is BELOW the capability default still denies — proving `max_tier` was not fed through as a floor (ADR-003 §3)
- [ ] Approval nonce path delegates to `@oikonomos/approvals`; no local reimplementation of consume (N8, N10)

### TASK-017
**Title:** OIK-028/029/030 — broker: idempotency, fail-closed, capability kill switch ⚑ protected
**Status:** pending
**Assigned_To:** TBD
**Priority:** critical
**Spec_References:** specs/OIKONOMOS_BUILD_DIRECTIVE_v1.0.md §4; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E3 OIK-028, OIK-029, OIK-030; docs/decisions/ADR-001-broker-enforcement-point.md R2, R3, CAN-04, CAN-08
**Owned_Paths:** packages/broker/src/**, packages/broker/test/**, packages/broker/vitest.config.ts
**Depends_On:** TASK-016
**Description:** Backlog (**GB or CX only, never S5**). The three properties that make the broker trustworthy under failure, sequenced behind TASK-016 in the same territory. (1) OIK-028 idempotency per `toolUseId`: L1 and L3 hitting the broker for the same call produce **one** audit event and one decision (ADR-001 R2, CAN-08) — the decision must be replayed, not recomputed, or a policy change mid-call could yield two different answers for one tool use. (2) OIK-029 fail-closed: timeout >10s, HTTP 500, and a malformed body each deny **and** audit (ADR-001 R3, CAN-04) — an unreachable broker that fails open is the whole threat model realised. (3) OIK-030 kill switch: `capabilities.enabled=false` denies immediately with no restart required, so the switch must be read per request rather than cached at startup.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] Same `toolUseId` via both L1 and L3 ⇒ exactly one audit event and one decision, with the second call replaying the first (WBS OIK-028; ADR-001 R2, CAN-08)
- [ ] Timeout >10s, 500, and malformed body each ⇒ deny plus an audit event, tested independently (WBS OIK-029; ADR-001 R3, CAN-04)
- [ ] `capabilities.enabled=false` denies immediately, verified without restarting the process (WBS OIK-030 "no restart required")
- [ ] TASK-016's contract tests still pass unchanged

**Review_Findings:**
- REWORK (ORCH opus-4-8, 2026-08-15T13:20Z; protected path infra/ci, author CX / reviewer ORCH-opus, different-model rule satisfied). The implementation is genuinely good and nearly all of it passes: territory CLEAN (single commit a739c74 [TASK-018], 7 files all in Owned_Paths + own dossier, zero PLAN.md commits on branch; PLAN.md appears in the diff only as base drift and is touched by no commit); the gate itself is correct and I proved it fires in both directions in a repo it had never seen; CODEOWNERS covers all six protected paths from CLAUDE.md/Handover §3 with none omitted, and additionally self-protects `.github/CODEOWNERS` and `.github/workflows/**`, which is a good instinct nobody asked for.
- [BLOCKING — the gate is DORMANT, exactly the TASK-007 defect] `infra/ci/run-local.mjs:56` invokes ONLY `test-protected-path-review.mjs` (the self-test). It never invokes `protected-path-review.mjs` — the actual gate — against a real diff. The only place the real gate runs is `.github/workflows/ci.yml:81`, guarded by `if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'`, and THIS REPOSITORY HAS NO REMOTE. Net effect: nothing in any automated path ever evaluates a protected diff. A protected-path change can be committed and merged today with no gate firing, which is the precise outcome WBS OIK-003 ("Protected-path PR without review is blocked by CI") exists to prevent. This task's own AC4 requires the README to state that "the local script is the operative control until then" — but an operative control that a human must remember to invoke by hand is not a control. This is the same finding, in the same shape, that sent TASK-007 back: a threshold was configured, was correct, failed closed when invoked, and nothing invoked it. It was rework then and it is rework now; approving it here would retire that precedent three tasks after I set it. FIX, small and fully in-territory (`infra/ci/**` is yours): have `run-local.mjs` run the real gate, not just its self-test. Note the design wrinkle and solve it deliberately rather than by suppressing the check — run-local is itself frequently executed on branches that legitimately touch protected paths (this very task's branch does), so a naive unconditional run would fail the local suite for exactly the tasks that need it most. Reasonable shapes: resolve the base as the merge-base with the integration branch and pass the marker through, or report a protected-diff hit as a distinct non-zero-with-explanation state that a marker argument clears. Choose one and say in the README which control is operative in a no-remote checkout.
- [NON-BLOCKING — record, do not fix here] The approval marker is SELF-ASSERTED. In CI it comes from grepping the PR body for `<!-- oikonomos-fable-review: approved -->`, which the PR author can paste into their own PR body; there is no reviewer-identity or cryptographic binding. Directive §3 requires adversarial review by a DIFFERENT MODEL on protected paths, and this layer cannot enforce that — CODEOWNERS plus branch protection is what would, and both are explicitly deferred until a remote exists. The script's header already documents this honestly, which is why it is not a rework item; it is recorded so the limitation is not later mistaken for enforcement.
- [NON-BLOCKING — decision for ORCH, not rework] `.claude/**` is only partially covered: CODEOWNERS and the gate's PROTECTED_PATHS cover `.claude/settings*.json`, `.claude/agents/**` and `.codex/**`, but not `.claude/commands/**`, `.claude/hooks/**` or `.claude/skills/**`. AC1 is strictly MET — CLAUDE.md's protected list says "all settings and subagent configs", which those three patterns do cover — but slash commands and hooks are executable control-plane surface, and the DEVDEPARTMENT territory firewall names `.claude/**` wholesale. ORCH to decide: widen both lists to `.claude/**`, or write down why the narrower carve-out is deliberate.
- APPROVED on RE-REVIEW (ORCH opus-4-8, 2026-08-15T14:30Z; protected path infra/ci, author CX / reviewer ORCH-opus, different-model rule satisfied). THE BLOCKING FINDING IS GENUINELY CLOSED, NOT PAPERED OVER. run-local.mjs now invokes the REAL gate (protected-path-review.mjs), not merely its self-test, and I checked all four ways it could have been rendered toothless - every one is closed: the base is git merge-base HEAD against the integration ref, so the diff is non-empty on any task branch (not HEAD, which would always be empty); the try/catch pushes status 1, making an unresolvable base a FAIL rather than a skip (fail-closed); there is no env-var gate and no default marker, since approvalMarker defaults undefined and any value other than the exact string is rejected at parse time; and the job's status is counted into the process exit code. THE STRONGEST EVIDENCE IS BEHAVIOURAL: node infra/ci/run-local.mjs on this very branch EXITS 1, correctly naming the six protected files the branch itself touches. CX left his own work failing his own gate rather than wiring an auto-marker or a self-exemption to make it green, which is the tell that the control is real. Bidirectionality re-proven by ORCH in a throwaway repo the builder's harness had never seen: protected diff without marker exit 1, same diff with the fable-reviewed marker exit 0, WRONG marker exit 1 (exact match, not truthiness), unprotected-only diff exit 0. BOTH ADDED CRITERIA MET: CODEOWNERS and PROTECTED_PATHS are now identical 11-entry lists, both covering hooks/**, .claude/** as a WHOLE TREE (not narrowed to settings*.json plus agents/**) and .codex/** - and CX went beyond the ask by adding a self-test assertion that mechanically holds the two lists in sync, so future drift fails CI instead of rotting silently. All other suites green (protected-path 8/8, banned-modes 8/8, secret-scan 11/11, both real scanners exit 0). Territory clean across both commits, zero PLAN.md commits. Merged --no-ff b360999. ORCH FOLLOW-UP, NOT A DEFECT IN THIS TASK: run-local.mjs now exits 1 on every protected-path branch until a human passes the marker. That is correct fail-closed semantics, but any automation treating run-local exit 0 as "ready to merge" will now block protected work permanently, and conversely an automation that passes the marker unconditionally would defeat the gate entirely. I own autopilot.json and must verify its review_cmd supplies the marker ONLY after the different-model adversarial review has actually run. It satisfies the review standard, which accepts a Progress_Note, and it does name every Owned_Paths entry with its state - recorded as a consistency preference, not a violation.

### TASK-021
**Title:** OIK-003 follow-on — control liveness gate (ADR-005) ⚑ protected
**Status:** claimed
**Assigned_To:** CX
**Priority:** high
**Spec_References:** docs/decisions/ADR-005-control-liveness.md; docs/decisions/ADR-002-permission-bypass-ban-scope.md Amendment B; docs/architecture/OIKONOMOS_Master_Work_Breakdown_v1.0.md §5 E1 OIK-003
**Owned_Paths:** infra/ci/**
**Depends_On:** TASK-018
**Description:** **GB or CX only, never S5** (protected path infra/ci). Implements ADR-005. Seven controls in this repo were found configured-but-inert in one day, and three of the seven were caught by luck rather than by any mechanism. The shared property is that a working control and an inert one present IDENTICAL symptoms: config present, command exits 0, output plausible. ATLAS is the sharpest case — `pack` opens the index on every dispatch, updating its mtime, so it looked freshly written at the exact second of each dispatch while its contents were six hours and eight merges stale. Add a `controls-live` job to `infra/ci/run-local.mjs` asserting LIVENESS — that each control actually ran — as distinct from correctness, which the existing jobs already cover. **Read ADR-005 §2 before writing anything: each assertion must key on evidence the control emits BY DOING ITS JOB (a recorded scan timestamp, a drained queue, a coverage report, a rejected commit) and NEVER on the presence of config, a file's mtime, or an exit code from a command that may have no-opped.** An assertion keyed on an mtime would reproduce the exact ATLAS failure one layer up. Where a control cannot emit such evidence, say so in the README rather than inventing a proxy. Keep every check able to FAIL: a liveness assertion that cannot fail is the thing this task exists to eliminate.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] `run-local.mjs` gains a `controls-live` job whose failure fails the run, covering at minimum: (a) ATLAS index freshness — the RECORDED scan timestamp (`atlas.py status`), not `atlas.db`'s mtime, plus indexed-file-count against tracked-file-count within a stated tolerance; (b) the territory pre-commit hook is installed and is ours (`scripts/install_git_hooks.ps1 -Verify` already reports this); (c) `.devteam/control/*.json` is empty, i.e. no undrained control blocks; (d) every unit in `autopilot.json`'s `builders.active` has a non-null `model`; (e) `packages/policy` coverage is actually COLLECTED on an ordinary test run, not merely configured
- [ ] Each assertion is proven able to FAIL — a self-test induces the inert state for every check and asserts a non-zero result. This is the criterion that matters: a liveness check that cannot fail is indistinguishable from no check
- [ ] No assertion keys on a file mtime, on the presence of a config key alone, or on an exit code from a command that may have no-opped (ADR-005 §2). State in the README, per check, WHICH emitted evidence it reads
- [ ] Staleness is reported in judgeable units — commits behind, files missing — not a bare timestamp the reader must date-arithmetic (ADR-005 §5)
- [ ] **ADDED 2026-08-15T16:20Z (ORCH) - sixth check, found during TASK-015 review and a textbook instance of the class this task exists for.** Assert that no workspace package's compiled `dist/` is STALE relative to its `src/`. Why: `@oikonomos/db`'s `exports` map resolves to `./dist/index.js` and `dist/` is gitignored, so cross-package tests execute build output rather than source. Demonstrated consequence - a mutation to `packages/db/src/approvals.ts` went entirely UNDETECTED by the approvals suite until a rebuild, and moving `packages/db/dist` aside makes 10 of 13 approvals test files fail to load. A suite that appears to guard `src` while executing `dist` is exactly the configured-but-inert shape of ADR-005. Key the assertion on evidence, per ADR-005 section 2: compare newest source mtime against build-output mtime per package, or verify the build is current, and FAIL when a package's dist is older than its src. Report it in judgeable terms (which package, how far behind), not a bare timestamp.
- [ ] `infra/ci/README.md` documents the job, and states plainly that liveness checks are themselves controls that can rot
- [ ] Existing `run-local.mjs` jobs and the infra/ci self-tests stay green; the protected-path gate still fails this branch without a marker
**Branch:** task/TASK-021-cx

**Assigned_To:** GB
**Priority:** critical

**Depends_On:** TASK-009
**Description:** Protocol §4 single-owner integration task, created at the 2026-08-15T14:05Z status scan because TASK-011 (S5) and TASK-013 (CX) both blocked OWNERSHIP_CONFLICT within minutes of each other on the same true finding: `packages/db` exposes no write path for `audit_events` or `approvals`, and OIK-014 ("no raw SQL outside packages/db") forbids their packages from working around it. TASK-009 solved manifest contention; this task solves API-surface readiness, the other half of the same lesson. **Deliver exactly three things and nothing more.** (1) `packages/db/src/auditEvents.ts` — `insertAuditEvent(event): Promise<AuditEvent>`, an INSERT against `audit_events` returning the persisted row. The table is append-only at the database level via TASK-002's `DO INSTEAD NOTHING` rules; do not attempt update/delete helpers, and do not swallow write errors — surface them to the caller, because TASK-011 must be able to fail its action closed on a write failure (ADR-001 R3). (2) `packages/db/src/approvals.ts` — `insertApproval(approval): Promise<Approval>` and `getApprovalByNonce(nonce): Promise<Approval | null>`. (3) the barrel exports in `packages/db/src/index.ts`. **DO NOT WRITE `consumeApproval`.** That statement belongs to TASK-014 (OIK-022, N8), which owns `packages/db/src/approvals.ts` in its own Owned_Paths and authors the atomic consume together with the concurrent-connection test that proves it — splitting that invariant from its proof is exactly how a fail-open defect survives review. Columns must match `infra/postgres/migrations/001_schema_v1.up.sql` verbatim; a §5.1 ambiguity is a SPEC_AMBIGUITY block, not a judgment call. Follow the existing `packages/db/src/database.ts` conventions: parameterised SQL only, typed row mappers, no credentials, no `any`. Do not add a runtime dependency — `pg` already lives in this package.
**Acceptance_Criteria:**
- [ ] `insertAuditEvent` persists a row whose columns match migration 001 verbatim, and returns the persisted row (not the input echoed back)
- [ ] A write failure from `insertAuditEvent` propagates to the caller as a rejected promise — proven by a test that induces a failure, not by inspection (ADR-001 R3; TASK-011 depends on this contract)
- [ ] `insertApproval` and `getApprovalByNonce` round-trip an approval carrying digest, render, destination, nonce and expiry (Handover §4.3 field set; Synthesis §5.1 column set)
- [ ] `getApprovalByNonce` returns null for an unknown nonce rather than throwing
- [ ] NO `consumeApproval` (or any status-mutating approval function) exists in this task's diff — reserved for TASK-014 (N8)
- [ ] All SQL is parameterised; `pnpm lint`, `pnpm -r typecheck`, `pnpm -r build` exit 0; integration tests pass against the compose Postgres and skip cleanly without `DATABASE_URL` (TASK-006 precedent)

Cause (measured, not inferred): Windows PowerShell 5.1 does not escape embedded double quotes when building a native command line, so the 3392-char prompt with its 42 quotes was re-split by grok's argv parser into two entries — `-p` consumed the first, the second arrived as a positional PROMPT, and grok refused the combination. Probed with an argv stub on the real prompt: ARGC=6 unescaped vs ARGC=5 escaped. Content-dependent, which is why GB's four previous dispatches succeeded. FIXED in dispatch.ps1 ($PromptArg, all three call sites, escaping verified content-preserving) and GB re-dispatched at 12:53Z on the existing claim — resume path, not a re-claim.
